\nu(\widetilde{X})|_{Q_{j}|_{x}}
is isomorphic to the normal bundle of
Q_{j}|_{x}=\mathbb{CP}^{1}
in
\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{x}=\mathbb{CP}^{2}
. This is the restriction to
Q_{x}=\mathbb{CP}^{1}
of the hyperplane bundle of
\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{x}=\mathbb{CP}^{2}
, i.e, the dual of the tautological line bundle
\mathcal{S}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{x}}\to\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{x}
. By our previous discussion and equation (
22
),

c_{1}(\mathcal{S}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{x}})=\frac{1}{2}[\upsilon_{j,\bar{0}}]|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{x}}:

\lambda_{j}[\tau_{j}]|_{Q_{j}|_{x}}=c_{1}(\nu(\tilde{X})|_{Q_{j}|_{x}})=-c_{1}(\mathcal{S}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{0}}|_{x}})|_{Q_{j}|_{x}}=-\frac{1}{2}[\tau_{j}]|_{Q_{j}|_{x}}.

The conclussion follows from
[\tau_{j}]|_{Q_{j}|_{x}}\neq 0
.
∎

We finally prove that
p_{1}(\widetilde{X})
depends on the classes
p_{1}(M)
,
[(1-g_{j})\mathrm{pr}_{j}^{*}\omega_{j}\wedge\tau_{j}]
, and
PD[L_{j}]
. Of course,
\iota^{*}(p_{1}(M))=p_{1}(M)
because

\iota
is a diffeomorphism. Hence, it induces a cohomology class on
X
.

Proposition 26
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
14
, we have:

p_{1}(\widetilde{X})=\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))+\sum_{j}{-3\rho^{*}(\mathrm{Th}[L_{j}])+(4-4g_{j})[\mathrm{pr}_{j}^{*}\omega_{j}\wedge\tau_{j}}].

Proof.

We first note that
p_{1}(\mathcal{M})=p_{1}(M)
.
We now claim that
p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}}=\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}}
. Since

\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}\cong\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{L}
,
we have

p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q}}=\rho^{*}(p_{1}(\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{L})).

(27)

Since the quotient map

q\colon\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{L}
is a cover, we have

q^{*}(T(\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{L}))=T(\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{L})
. Thus,

p_{1}(\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{L})=q_{*}(p_{1}(\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{L}))=q_{*}(p_{1}(\mathcal{M})|_{\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{L}})=p_{1}(M)|_{\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{L}},

(28)

and equations (
27
), (
28
) imply the claim. The long exact sequence for the relative cohomology of the pair

(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}},\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q})
ensures that

p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})-\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))\in H^{4}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}},\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}-\mathcal{Q})
. Lemma
21
implies

p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})-\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))=\sum_{j}[\mathrm{pr}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{*}\beta_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}\wedge\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}],\qquad[\beta_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}]\in H^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}),

(29)

where
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathcal{Q}}\colon\sqcup_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}B_{\delta}(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}})\to\mathcal{Q}
denotes the nearest point projection.

We now focus on the restriction of
p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})-\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))
to
{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}
. First, from the short exact sequence (
23
) we obtain
\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}=0
.
In addition,

0\to T\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}\to T\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}\to\nu(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}})\to 0

yields
p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}=p_{1}(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}})+p_{1}(\nu(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}))
.
Equations (
19
), (
22
)
imply

p_{1}(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}})=\left[\frac{3}{4}\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}^{2}-(2-2g_{j})\mathrm{pr}^{*}\omega_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}\wedge\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}\right]\bigg|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}.

Since
c_{1}(\nu(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}))=[\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}]|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}
, we have
p_{1}(\nu(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}))=[\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}^{2}]|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}
.
Hence,
p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}=\frac{7}{4}[\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}^{2}]|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}-(2-2g_{j})[\mathrm{pr}^{*}\omega_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}\wedge\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}]|_{\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}}
.
Proposition
23
and equation (
22
) guarantee that
the restriction map
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{*}H^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}})\wedge[\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}]\to H^{4}(\mathcal{Q}_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}})
is injective. Thus,

p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})-\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))=\sum_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}{\frac{7}{4}[\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}^{2}]-(2-2g_{j})[\mathrm{pr}^{*}\omega_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}\wedge\upsilon_{j,\bar{\varepsilon}}]}.

(30)

In addition, from

0\to T\widetilde{X}\to T\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}|_{\widetilde{X}}\to\nu(\widetilde{X})\to 0,

we deduce
p_{1}(\widetilde{X})=p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})|_{X}-p_{1}(\nu(\widetilde{X}))
. From
equations (
21
) and (
30
) we deduce

p_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}})|_{\widetilde{X}}=\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))+\sum_{j}{-\frac{7}{2}\rho^{*}(\mathrm{Th}[L_{j}])+(5-5g_{j})\mathrm{pr}^{*}\omega_{j}\wedge\tau_{j}}.

(31)

Finally, from Lemma
25
we obtain

p_{1}(\nu(\widetilde{X}))=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j}[\tau_{j}^{2}]=\sum_{j}{-\frac{1}{2}\rho^{*}(\mathrm{Th}[L_{j}])+(1-g_{j})[\mathrm{pr}^{*}\omega_{j}\wedge\tau_{j}]},

(32)

The statement follows subtracting (
32
) from (
31
).
∎

Remark 9
.

We cannot compute directly
p_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))
using the short exact sequence

0\to TQ_{j}\to T\widetilde{X}|_{L_{j}\times\mathbb{CP}^{1}}\to\nu(Q_{j}).

A similar argument shows

p_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))=\sum_{j}[\mathrm{pr}_{Q}^{*}(\beta_{j})\wedge\tau_{j}],\qquad[\beta_{j}]\in H^{2}(Q_{j}),

where
\mathrm{pr}_{Q}\colon\sqcup B_{\delta}(Q_{j})\to Q
denotes the nearest point projection. In the same way,
\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))|_{Q_{j}}=0
. In addition,
using the short exact sequence

0\to TQ_{j}\to T\widetilde{X}|_{Q_{j}}\to\nu(Q_{j})\to 0
,
equation (
19
), and that
c_{1}(\nu(Q_{j}))=\tau_{j}
one obtains:

p_{1}(\widetilde{X})|_{Q_{j}}=[\tau_{j}^{2}]|_{Q_{j}}=(4-4g_{j})[\mathrm{pr}^{*}(\omega_{j})\wedge\tau_{j}]|_{Q_{j}}.

We cannot deduce
p_{1}(\widetilde{X})-\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))
from this because the restriction map
\mathrm{pr}_{Q}^{*}(H^{2}(Q_{j}))\wedge[\tau_{j}]\to H^{4}(Q_{j})
is not injective. Increasing the codimension of the singular locus fixes that.

Remark 10
.

If we instead construct the resolution using
\theta\in\Omega^{1}(L)
, closed and nowhere-vanishing, and use
the notations of Remark
8
, we have

p_{1}(\widetilde{X})=\rho^{*}(p_{1}(M))+\sum_{j}{-3\,\rho^{*}(\mathrm{Th}[L_{j}])+2\,c_{1}(\ker(\theta|_{L_{j}}))\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}}.

4
Equivariant
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-resolutions

In this section we prove Theorem
2
. For this, we assume that
(M,\varphi,g)
is a compact manifold eqquiped with a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure, and that
\iota\colon M\to M
is an involution satisfying
\iota^{*}\varphi=\varphi
and
L=\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)\neq\emptyset
. We consider the orbifold
X=M/\iota
.
We further assume the existence of a nowhere-vanishing closed
1
-form
\theta\in\Omega^{1}(L)
, a finite order diffeomorphism
\kappa\colon M\to M
and a continuous function
\sigma\colon L\to\{\pm 1\}
such that

\kappa^{*}(\varphi)=\varphi,\quad\iota\circ\kappa=\kappa\circ\iota,\quad\kappa^{*}(\theta)=\sigma\theta.

4.1
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure on the normal bundle

Let
\pi\colon\nu(L)\to L
be the normal bundle. Consider
R>0
such that the neighborhood
\nu_{R}(L)=\{v_{p}\in\nu(L)\colon\,\|v_{p}\|<R\}
is diffeomorphic to a neighborhood
\mathcal{U}
of
L
in
M
via the exponential map; we identify these. Then,
\phi=\exp_{L}^{*}\varphi
is a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form on

\nu_{R}(L)
, and it induces the metric
\exp_{L}^{*}g
, which we also call
g
. The maps
\iota
and
\kappa
induce vector bundle homomorphisms of
\nu(L)
via their differentials, which we continue to denote by
\iota
and
\kappa
.

Let
\nabla
be a connection on
\nu(L)
such that both
I
and the hermitian metric determined by
I
and
g
are parallel with respect to
\nabla
. This condition will be needed to define the
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure in the resolution (see subsection
4.4
). The connection yields a splitting

T\nu(L)=V\oplus H
where
V=\ker(d\pi)

and an isomorphism of vector bundles
\mathcal{T}\colon T\nu(L)\longmapsto\pi^{*}(TM|_{L})
. More precisely, if
u_{p}\in T_{p}L
and
w_{p},v_{p}\in\nu_{p}(L)
, then
u_{p}+w_{p}\in\pi^{*}(TM|_{L})_{v_{p}}
, and

\mathcal{T}^{-1}(u_{p}+w_{p})=(d\pi|_{H_{v_{p}}})^{-1}(u_{p})+\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}{(v_{p}+tw_{p})}.

(33)

The splitting of
T\nu(L)
induces a decomposition
T^{*}\nu(L)=V^{*}\oplus H^{*}
, where
V^{*}=\mathrm{Ann}(H)
and
H^{*}=\mathrm{Ann}(V)=\pi^{*}(T^{*}L)
, which yields

\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\nu(L)=\oplus_{p+q=k}W^{k}_{p,q},\qquad W^{k}_{p,q}=\Lambda^{p}V^{*}\otimes\Lambda^{q}H^{*}.

(34)

Given
\beta\in\Omega^{k}(\nu(L))
, we denote its projection to
W^{k}_{p,q}
as
[\beta]_{p,q}
. The complex structure on
\nu(L)

I(v_{p})=\frac{1}{\|\theta_{p}\|}\theta^{\sharp}_{p}\times v_{p},

(35)

defined similarly to equation (
13
) satisfies
I\circ\kappa=\sigma I\circ\kappa
because

\kappa^{*}\varphi=\varphi
, and
\kappa^{*}\theta=\sigma\theta
.
We extend
I
as a complex structure on
V
; if
v_{p}\in\nu_{p}(L)
then

I\left(\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}{(v_{p}+tw_{p})}\right)=\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}{(v_{p}+tI(w_{p}))}.

In particular, since
\kappa_{*}(V)=V
, we have

\kappa_{*}|_{V}\circ I=\sigma I\circ\kappa_{*}|_{V}
.
Similarly, we extend
I
to
V^{*}
by pullback. That is, we set
I\alpha(X)=\alpha(IX)
if
X\in V
. The
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure on
\nu(L)
given by
\phi_{1}=\mathcal{T}^{*}(\pi^{*}(\varphi|_{TM|_{L}}))
determines the metric
g_{1}=\mathcal{T}^{*}(\pi^{*}g|_{TM|_{L}})
. This makes the summands of the decomposition (
34
) orthogonal to each other.

The radius function
r\colon\nu(L)\to[0,\infty)
,
r(v_{p})=g(v_{p},v_{p})^{\frac{1}{2}}
, is
\iota
- and
\kappa
-invariant.
We denote dilations of ratio
t
by
F_{t}\colon\nu(L)\to\nu(L)
,
F_{t}(v_{p})=tv_{p}
.
We observe that
\iota=F_{-1}
, while
\kappa
satisfies
F_{t}\circ\kappa=\kappa\circ F_{t}
because
\kappa
is a bundle homomorphism.
The dilations allow us to find the Taylor expansions of
F_{t}^{*}\phi
and
F_{t}^{*}g
near
t=0
; that is,

F_{t}^{*}(\phi)\sim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{t^{2k}\phi^{2k}},\quad F_{t}^{*}g\sim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{t^{2k}g^{2k}},

(36)

where the even terms vanish because
\iota=F_{-1}
. In
[
29
]
we also prove that:

1.

\|[\phi^{2k}]_{p,q}\|_{g_{1}}=O(r^{2k-p})
and
[\phi^{2k}]_{p,q}=0
if
p>2k
.

2.

The forms
\phi^{2k}
are closed, and if
k\geq 1
then
\phi^{2k}=\frac{1}{2k}d(i(\mathcal{R})\phi^{2k})
, where
\mathcal{R}(v_{p})=\frac{d}{dt}\mid_{t=0}(e^{t}v_{p})
.

From
F_{t}\circ\kappa=\kappa\circ F_{t}
, we also deduce
\kappa^{*}\phi^{2k}=\phi^{2k}
and
\kappa^{*}g^{2k}=g^{2k}
.
These properties imply the estimate
\phi-\phi^{0}-\phi^{2}=O(r)
, which allows us to interpolate
\phi
with the closed form
\phi_{2}=\phi^{0}+\phi^{2}
. The following result is the
\kappa
-equivariant version of
[
29
, Proposition 3.5]
, and it can be proved similarly.

Proposition 27
.

There exists
0<\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{R}{4}
such that for each
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
there is a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form
\phi_{3,\varepsilon}\in\Omega^{3}(M)
, invariant under both
\iota
and
\kappa
, satisfying
\phi_{3,\varepsilon}=\phi_{2}
if
r\leq 2\varepsilon
and
\phi_{3,\varepsilon}=\phi
if
r\geq 4\varepsilon
.

4.2
Average of forms

While
\kappa(V)=V
, there is no reason to expect that
\kappa(H)=H
in general. However, this is true along the zero section
Z\subset\nu(L)
, as
H_{0_{p}}=T_{0_{p}}Z
and
\kappa
preserves
Z
.
In addition, the forms introduced in
[
29
]
are not
\kappa
-invariant and we need to average them. We therefore introduce the following notation:

\mu(\alpha)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}{(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\alpha},\qquad\alpha\in\Omega^{*}(\nu(L)),

where
N
is the order of
\kappa
. Since
\kappa
and
\iota
commute, the average of an
\iota
-invariant form is both
\iota
- and
\kappa
-invariant. The following Lemmas allow us to estimate the errors introduced when we average forms.

Lemma 28
.

Let
0\leq r\leq k
. If
\beta\in\oplus_{p=0}^{r}{W_{p,k-p}}
,
then
(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\beta\in\oplus_{p=0}^{r}{W_{p,k-p}}
and

[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\beta]_{r,k-r}=[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}[\beta]_{r,k-r}]_{r,k-r}.

Hence,
\mu(\beta)\in\oplus_{p=0}^{r}{W_{p,k-p}}
and

[\mu(\beta)]_{r,k-r}=[\mu([\beta]_{r,k-r})]_{r,k-r}
.

Proof.

We prove the first statement for
r=0
and
k=1
. If
\gamma\in W_{0,1}
, then

\gamma=\sum_{j}f_{j}\pi^{*}\alpha_{j}
,
for some
f_{j}\in C^{\infty}(\nu(L))
, and
\alpha_{j}\in\Omega^{1}(L).

Since

\kappa^{*}\pi^{*}\alpha_{j}=\pi^{*}\kappa^{*}\alpha_{j}
we have

(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}(\gamma)=\sum_{j}{(f_{j}\circ\kappa^{\ell})\,\pi^{*}((\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\alpha_{j})}\in H^{*}.

Of course,
[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\gamma]_{0,1}=(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\gamma=[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}[\gamma]_{0,1}]_{0,1}
. The case
r=k=1
follows from linearity. That is, if
\beta\in\Omega^{1}(\nu(L))
, then

[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\beta]_{1,0}=[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}[\beta]_{1,0}]_{1,0}+[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}[\beta]_{0,1}]_{1,0}=[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}[\beta]_{1,0}]_{1,0}
. For the general case, observe that if
\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{p}\in V^{*}
and
\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{q}\in H^{*}
, the previous arguments ensure:

\displaystyle[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}(\beta_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\beta_{p}\wedge\gamma_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\gamma_{q})]_{p,q}=

\displaystyle\,[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\beta_{1}]_{1,0}\wedge\dots\wedge[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\beta_{p}]_{1,0}\wedge(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\gamma_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\gamma_{q},

\displaystyle[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}(\beta_{1}\wedge\dots\beta_{p}\wedge\gamma_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\gamma_{q})]_{s,p+q-s}=

\displaystyle\,0,\mbox{ if }s>p.

This implies the general case, and the second statement follows from this by averaging.
∎

Lemma 29
.

Define
e_{1}=\|\theta\|^{-1}\theta
and let
\alpha\in\Omega^{1}(\nu(L))
, then

1.

[d\alpha]_{2,0}=[d[\alpha]_{1,0}]_{2,0}
.

2.

If
\alpha\in V^{*}
then
[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}I\alpha]_{1,0}=\sigma^{\ell}I[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\alpha]_{1,0}
.

3.

Let
f\colon\nu(L)\to\mathbb{R}
be a
\kappa
-invariant function on
\nu(L)
, then

[\mu(\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[df]_{1,0})]_{1,1}=\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[df]_{1,0}
,
and

[\mu(\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[df]_{1,0})]_{2,1}=\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge[dI[df]_{1,0}]_{2,0}
.

Proof.

If
\alpha\in\Omega^{1}(\nu(L))
, then

[\alpha]_{0,1}=\sum_{j}f_{j}\pi^{*}\alpha_{j}
with
\alpha_{j}\in\Omega^{1}(L)
and
f_{j}\in C^{\infty}(\nu(L))
. Hence,

d[\alpha]_{0,1}=\sum_{j}[df_{j}]_{1,0}\wedge\pi^{*}\alpha_{j}+\sum_{j}[df_{j}]_{0,1}\wedge\pi^{*}\alpha_{j}+f_{j}\pi^{*}(d\alpha_{j})\in W_{1,1}\oplus W_{0,2}.

So
[d[\alpha]_{0,1}]_{2,0}=0
. By linearity,

[d\alpha]_{2,0}=[d[\alpha]_{1,0}]_{2,0}+[d[\alpha]_{0,1}]_{2,0}=[d[\alpha]_{1,0}]_{2,0}
.

Assume that
\alpha\in V^{*}
, and let
X\in V
. The equality
\kappa_{*}|_{V}\circ I=\sigma I\circ\kappa_{*}|_{V}
yields:

[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}I\alpha]_{1,0}(X)=\alpha((I(\kappa^{\ell}_{*}X))=\sigma^{\ell}\alpha(\kappa^{\ell}_{*}(IX))=\sigma^{\ell}[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\alpha]_{1,0}(IX)=\sigma^{\ell}I[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\alpha]_{1,0}(X).

therefore,
[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}I\alpha]_{1,0}=\sigma^{\ell}I[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\alpha]_{1,0}
.

Let
f
be a
\kappa
-invariant function. Using the second statement and Lemma
28
, we obtain

[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}(I[df]_{1,0})]_{1,0}=\sigma^{\ell}I[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}[df]_{1,0}]_{1,0}=I[df]_{1,0}.

(37)

From
\kappa^{*}e_{1}=\sigma e_{1}
we deduce

[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}(\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[df]_{1,0})]_{1,1}=\sigma^{\ell}\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}I[df]_{1,0}]_{1,0}=\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[df]_{1,0}.

This ensures
[\mu(\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[df]_{1,0})]_{1,1}=\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[df]_{1,0}
. We finally define
\beta=\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[df]_{1,0}\in W_{2,1}\oplus W_{1,2}\oplus W_{0,3}
. The first statement and equation (
37
) show:

\displaystyle[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}(dI[df]_{1,0})]_{2,0}=

\displaystyle[d((\kappa^{\ell})^{*}I[df]_{1,0})]_{2,0}=[d[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}I[df]_{1,0}]_{1,0}]_{2,0}=\sigma^{\ell}[dI[df]_{1,0}]_{2,0}.

Therefore,

[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}\beta]_{2,1}=\sigma^{\ell}\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge[(\kappa^{\ell})^{*}(dI[df]_{1,0})]_{2,0}=\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge[(dI[df]_{1,0})]_{2,0}=[\beta]_{2,1}.

The stated equality follows from this.
∎

4.3
Local formulas for a family of
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structures

We collect in a Proposition some of the local formulas we found on
[
29
, Section 3.3.1]

Proposition 30
.

The following equalities hold:

1.

\phi_{1}=\phi^{0}+[\phi^{2}]_{2,1}

2.

g_{1}=g_{0,2}^{0}+g_{2,0}^{2}

In addition, let
e_{1}=\|\theta\|^{-1}\theta
and consider an orthonormal oriented frame
(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3})
of
T^{*}L
on a neighbourhood
U\subset L
. Then

\phi^{0}=\pi^{*}(e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\wedge e_{3}),\qquad[\phi^{2}]_{2,1}=\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge\omega_{1}+\pi^{*}e_{2}\wedge\omega_{2}-\pi^{*}e_{3}\wedge\omega_{3},

where
\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3}\in W_{2,0}
satisfy

\omega_{1}=-\frac{1}{4}[d[Idr^{2}]_{1,0}]_{2,0},\qquad\omega_{2}+i\omega_{3}=[d\vartheta]_{2,0},

and
\vartheta\in V^{*}\otimes\mathbb{C}

is a
\iota
-invariant
1
-form such that
d_{\nu_{p}(L)}(\vartheta|_{\nu_{p}(L)})=(\omega_{2}+i\omega_{3})|_{\nu_{p}(L)}
and
\vartheta|_{T\nu(L)|_{Z}}=0
.

Remark 11
.

In
[
29
]
,
\vartheta
is denoted by
\mu
.

Let
t>0
, the form
F_{t}^{*}\phi_{1}=\phi^{0}+t^{2}[\phi^{2}]_{2,1}
is a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure on
\nu(L)
with associated metric
g_{t}=F_{t}^{*}(g_{1})=g_{0,2}^{0}+t^{2}g_{2,0}^{2}
. Since
F_{t}^{*}\phi_{1}
might not be closed,
in
[
29
, Section 3.3.2]
we defined a family of closed
\iota
-invariant forms
\widehat{\phi}_{2}^{t}
on
\nu(L)
, close to
F_{t}^{*}\phi_{1}
, whose local formula is manageable. This is,

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi}_{2}^{t}=

\displaystyle\pi^{*}(e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\wedge e_{3})+t^{2}\beta,\qquad\qquad\beta=\beta_{1}+\beta_{2},

\displaystyle\beta_{1}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\pi^{*}\theta\wedge d((\|\theta\|^{-1}\circ\pi)I[dr^{2}]_{1,0}),\quad\beta_{2}=d(\pi^{*}e_{2}\wedge\vartheta_{2}-\pi^{*}e_{3}\wedge\vartheta_{3}).

Note that
\beta,\widehat{\phi}^{t}_{2}\in W_{2,1}\oplus W_{1,2}\oplus W_{0,3}
. From the definitions we deduce,

[\beta]_{2,1}=[\phi_{1}]_{2,1}=[\phi^{2}]_{2,1}.

(38)

Let
s>0
. Using equation (
38
) and the equality

\|\gamma\|_{g_{t}}=\frac{1}{t^{3}}\|[\gamma]_{3,0}\|_{g_{1}}+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\|[\gamma]_{2,1}\|_{g_{1}}+\frac{1}{t}\|[\gamma]_{1,2}\|_{g_{1}}+\|[\gamma]_{0,3}\|_{g_{1}}

for
\gamma\in\Omega^{3}(\nu(L))
, we deduce that
\|\widehat{\phi}_{2}^{t}-F_{t}^{*}\phi_{1}\|_{g_{t}}=O(t)
on
\nu_{2s}(L)
. From Lemma
12
we deduce that
\widehat{\phi}_{2}^{t}
determines a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure on
\nu_{2s}(L)
for small values of
t>0
. For our purposes, we need to show that
\mu(\widehat{\phi}^{t}_{2})
also does so, and then interpolate it with the
\iota
- and
\kappa
-invariant form

\phi_{2}^{t}=F_{t}^{*}(\phi_{2})=\phi^{0}+t^{2}\phi^{2}.

The following Lemma generalizes equation (
38
) and will help us to achieve this in Proposition
32
.

Lemma 31
.

The following equality holds:

[\mu(\beta)]_{2,1}=[\beta]_{2,1}=[\phi_{1}]_{2,1}=[\phi^{2}]_{2,1}.

(39)

In addition,
[\mu(\beta_{k})]_{2,1}=[\beta_{k}]_{2,1}
for
k=1,2
.

Proof.

Equation (
39
) is a consequence of Lemma
28
, equation (
38
),
\phi^{2}_{3,0}=0
, and
\kappa^{*}\phi^{2}=\phi^{2}
:

[\mu(\beta)]_{2,1}=[\mu([\beta]_{2,1})]_{2,1}=[\mu([\phi^{2}]_{2,1})]_{2,1}=[\phi^{2}]_{2,1}=[\beta]_{2,1}.

We now prove
[\mu(\beta_{1})]_{2,1}=[\beta_{1}]_{2,1}
.
Since
d(\|\theta\|\circ\pi)\in W_{0,1}
we have

[\beta_{1}]_{2,1}=-\frac{1}{4}\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge[dI[dr^{2}]_{1,0}]_{2,0}=-\frac{1}{4}[\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[dr^{2}]_{1,0}]_{2,1}
, and
\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[dr^{2}]_{1,0}\in W_{2,1}\oplus W_{1,2}\oplus W_{0,3}
. Lemmas
28
and
29
allow us to obtain:

\displaystyle[\mu(\beta_{1})]_{2,1}=

\displaystyle[\mu([\beta_{1}]_{2,1})]_{2,1}=-\frac{1}{4}[\mu([\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[dr^{2}]_{1,0}]_{2,1})]_{2,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}[\mu(\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[dr^{2}]_{1,0})]_{2,1}=-\frac{1}{4}\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge[dI[dr^{2}]_{1,0}]_{2,0}=[\beta_{1}]_{2,1}.

Finally,
[\mu(\beta_{2})]_{2,1}=[\mu(\beta-\beta_{1})]_{2,1}=[\beta-\beta_{1}]_{2,1}=[\beta_{2}]_{2,1}
.
∎

Proposition 32
.

Let
s>0
, there exists
t_{s}>0
such that for each
t<t_{s}
, there is a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form
\widehat{\phi}_{3,s}^{t}
on
\nu_{2s}(L)
which is invariant under
\iota
and
\kappa
, and satisfies

\widehat{\phi}_{3,s}^{t}|_{r\leq\frac{s}{4}}=\mu(\widehat{\phi}_{2}^{t})
and
\widehat{\phi}_{3,s}^{t}|_{r\geq\frac{s}{2}}=\phi_{2}^{t}
.

Proof.

In
[
29
, Proposition 3.7]
, we proved that there is a
\iota
-invariant form

\xi\in W_{0,2}
such that
\|\xi\|_{g_{1}}=O(r^{2})
, and
\phi^{2}=\beta+d\xi
. In particular, equation (
38
) implies
d\xi\in W_{1,2}\oplus W_{0,3}
.
Let
\varpi
be a smooth function such that
\varpi=0
if
x\leq\frac{1}{4}
and
\varpi=1
if
x\geq\frac{1}{2}
, and let
\varpi_{s}(x)=\varpi(\frac{x}{s})
. The form

\widehat{\phi}_{3,s}^{t}=\phi^{0}+t^{2}\mu(\beta)+t^{2}d(\varpi_{s}(r)\mu(\xi))

is closed,
\iota
- and
\kappa
-invariant, and it coincides with
\mu(\widehat{\phi}_{2}^{t})
on
r\leq\frac{s}{4}
and with
\mu(\phi_{2}^{t})=\phi_{2}^{t}
on
r\geq\frac{s}{2}
. We now check that this is a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form on
\nu_{2s}(L)
when
t>0
is small enough. For this, we compare
\widehat{\phi}{}^{t}_{3,s}
with
F_{t}^{*}\phi_{1}
and use Lemma
12
to conclude. Using Lemma
28
we obtain
d(\varpi_{s}\mu(\xi))\in W_{1,2}\oplus W_{0,3}
.
Equality (
39
) and Proposition
30
imply:

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\phi}_{3,s}^{t}-F_{t}^{*}\phi_{1}\|_{g_{t}}=

\displaystyle\,t\|[\mu(\beta)+d(\varpi_{s}(r)\mu(\xi))]_{1,2}\|_{g_{1}}+t^{2}\|[\mu(\beta)+d(\varpi_{s}(r)\mu(\xi))]_{0,3}\|_{g_{1}}.

Lemma
12
implies that
\widetilde{\phi}^{t}_{3,s}
is a

\mathrm{G}_{2}
form on
\nu_{2s}(L)
when
t<t_{s}
, and
t_{s}<1
satisfies

\mathrm{max}_{r\leq 2s}(\|[\mu(\beta)+d(\varpi_{s}(r)\mu(\xi))]_{1,2}\|_{g_{1}}+\|[\mu(\beta)+d(\varpi_{s}(r)\mu(\xi))]_{0,3}\|_{g_{1}})<\frac{m}{t_{s}}.

∎

4.4
The resolution of
\nu(L)/\iota

We now consider the bundle
P=P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\nu(L))\times_{\mathrm{U}(2)}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}^{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and the projection
\chi\colon P\to\nu(L)/\iota
. The exceptional divisor is
Q=\chi^{-1}(L)
.
We denote elements in
(P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\nu(L)))_{p}
as complex maps
F_{p}\colon\nu_{p}(L)\to\mathbb{C}^{2}
. Explicitly,
\chi[F_{p},[v,\ell]]=[(F_{p})^{-1}(v)]_{\iota}
.
It is also convenient to denote
\mathrm{pr}=\pi\circ\chi
.

Proposition 33
.

There is a diffeomorphism
\tilde{\kappa}\colon P\to P
such that
\kappa\circ\chi=\chi\circ\tilde{\kappa}
.

Proof.

Given
L_{j}
, we set
L_{k}=\kappa(L_{j})
(possibly
j=k
), and we define the extension
\tilde{\kappa}\colon P|_{L_{j}}\to P|_{L_{k}}
.
We first assume
\sigma|_{L_{j}}=1
, case in which
\kappa\colon\nu(L_{j})\to\nu(L_{k})
is a complex homomorphism. There is a induced equivariant morphism
\kappa\colon P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\nu(L_{j}))\to P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\nu(L_{k}))
covering
\kappa\colon L_{j}\to L_{k}
, namely

\kappa(F_{p})=F_{p}\circ(\kappa|_{p})^{-1}\in(P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\nu(L_{k})))_{\kappa(p)},

where
\kappa|_{p}\colon\nu_{p}(L_{j})\to\nu_{\kappa(p)}(L_{k})
is the restriction of
\kappa
to
\nu_{p}(L)
.
Since
P=P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\nu(L))\times_{U(2)}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}^{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, this induces

\tilde{\kappa}[F_{p},[v,\ell]]=[\kappa(F_{p}),[v,\ell]]
, which is the claimed extension as:

(\chi\circ\tilde{\kappa})[F_{p},[v,\ell]]=[(F_{p}\circ(\kappa|_{p})^{-1})^{-1}(v)]_{\iota}=[\kappa_{p}(F_{p}^{-1}(v))]_{\iota}=(\kappa\circ\chi)[F_{p},[v,\ell]].

Suppose
\sigma|_{L_{j}}=-1
. Let
\overline{\nu(L_{j})}\to L
be the conjugate bundle of
\nu(L_{j})
; then

\kappa\colon\overline{\nu(L_{j})}\to\nu(L_{k})
is a complex homomorphism. We denote elements in
(P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\overline{\nu(L_{j})}))_{p}
as complex maps
G_{p}\colon\overline{\nu(L)}_{p}\to\mathbb{C}^{2}
.
Similarly, we define a map
\tilde{\kappa}^{\prime}\colon P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\overline{\nu(L_{j})})\times_{\mathrm{U}(2)}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}^{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to P|_{L_{k}}
,
\tilde{\kappa}^{\prime}[G_{p},[v,\ell]]=[G_{p}\circ(\kappa|_{p})^{-1},[v,\ell]]
.

Observe that the diffeomorphism

c\colon P|_{L_{j}}\to P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\overline{\nu(L_{j})})
,
c(F_{p})(w_{p})=\overline{F_{p}(w_{p})}

satisfies
c(A\cdot F_{p})=\overline{A}\cdot c(F_{p})
,
A\in\mathrm{U}(2)
.
In addition, since conjugation maps complex lines to complex lines, it induces a map
\bar{\cdot}\colon\widetilde{\mathbb{C}^{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to\widetilde{\mathbb{C}^{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. The diffeomorphism,

c\colon P|_{L_{j}}\to P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(\overline{\nu(L_{j})})\times_{\mathrm{U}(2)}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}^{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2},\quad[F_{p},[v,\ell]]\longmapsto[c(F_{p}),\overline{[v,\ell]}],

is well-defined because

c[A\cdot F_{p},A^{-1}[v,\ell]]=[\overline{A}\cdot F_{p},\overline{A}^{-1}\cdot\overline{[v,\ell]}]
when
A\in\mathrm{U}(2)
.
The extension is
\tilde{\kappa}=\tilde{\kappa}^{\prime}\circ c

because, using
c(F_{p})^{-1}(\bar{v})=F_{p}^{-1}(v)
(which follows from

c(F_{p})(F_{p}^{-1}(v))=\bar{v}
), we obtain:

(\chi\circ\tilde{\kappa})[F_{p},[v,\ell]]=[(c(F_{p})\circ(\kappa|_{p})^{-1})^{-1}(\bar{v})]_{\iota}=[\kappa|_{p}\circ c(F_{p})^{-1}(\bar{v})]_{\iota}=[\kappa_{p}(F_{p}^{-1}(v))]_{\iota}=(\kappa\circ\chi)[F_{p},[v,\ell]].

∎

As discussed in
[
29
]
,
\nabla
lifts to the bundle
P\to L
because
\nabla I=0
. The splitting
TP=V^{\prime}\oplus H^{\prime}
determined by
\nabla
is compatible with
T\nu(L)=V\oplus H
on
P-Q
. Namely, if
p\in P-Q
and
\chi(p)=[x]_{\iota}
, then
d\chi_{p}(H^{\prime}_{p})
is the image of
H_{x}
by the projection
T\nu(L)-Z\to(T\nu(L)-Z)/\iota
.
This induces a decomposition
\Lambda^{k}T^{*}P=\oplus_{i+j=k}\Lambda^{i}V^{\prime}\otimes\Lambda^{j}H^{\prime}
. We also denote by
[\alpha]_{i,j}
to the projection of
\alpha
to
\Lambda^{i}V^{\prime}\otimes\Lambda^{j}H^{\prime}
. Due to the compatibility condition on
P-Q
we have:

\chi^{*}([\alpha]_{p,q})=[\chi^{*}(\alpha)]_{p,q}.

Note that we view forms on
(\nu(L)-Z)/\iota
as
\iota
-invariant forms on
\nu(L)-Z
. In addition, there is a well-defined complex structure
I
on
(V^{\prime})^{*}
.
The radius function lifts to
P
; its fourth power
r^{4}
is smooth. Finally, Proposition
33
allows us to lift
\mu
to
P
as

\tilde{\mu}(\alpha)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}{(\tilde{\kappa}^{\ell})^{*}\alpha},

as the order of
\tilde{\kappa}
is also
N
.

We collect in a Proposition the formulas for the construction of a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure on
P
.

Proposition 34
.

[
29
, Section 3.4]

In the set-up of Proposition
30
, we denote
|\theta|=\|\theta\|\circ\mathrm{pr}
. Then,

1.

Let
\mathsf{f}_{a}(x)=\mathbf{g}_{a}(x)+2a\log(x)
, where
\mathbf{g}_{a}(x)=(x^{4}+a^{2})^{1/2}-a\log((x^{4}+a^{2})^{1/2}+a)
.
Then

\widehat{\omega}_{1}=-\frac{1}{4}d(|\theta|^{-1}I[d\mathsf{f}_{|\theta|}(r)]_{1,0}),

is smooth on
P
.

2.

The forms
\chi^{*}(\vartheta_{k}),\chi^{*}(\omega_{k})
,
k=2,3
are smooth on
P
.

3.

The form
\Phi_{1}=\mathrm{pr}^{*}(e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\wedge e_{3})+\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge[\widehat{\omega}_{1}]_{2,0}+\mathrm{pr}^{*}e_{2}\wedge\chi^{*}\omega_{2}+\mathrm{pr}^{*}e_{3}\wedge\chi^{*}\omega_{3}
determines a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure on
P
.

4.

The metric
h
associated to
\Phi_{1}
satisfies
h_{1,1}=0
. In addition,
\chi_{*}(h_{0,2})=g_{0,2}^{0}
and
\|\chi_{*}(h_{2,0})-g_{2,0}^{2}\|_{g_{0,2}^{2}}=O(r^{-2})
on
r>0
.

Remark 12
.

We verify that
dI[d\log(r^{2})]_{1,0}
is related to the curvature
R
of the connection on the tautological bundle
{\pi}_{1}\colon S\to Q
, determined by projecting to
S
the pullback connection of
\pi^{*}(\nu(L))\to Q
. More precisely,
dI[d\log(r^{2})]_{1,0}=2i\pi^{*}(R)
. Given a local unitary frame
(v_{1},v_{2})
of
\nu(L)
we write
\nabla_{X}v_{j}=A_{j}^{1}(X)v_{1}+A_{j}^{2}(X)v_{2}
, with
A_{j}^{j}(X)\in i\mathbb{R}
and
\bar{A}_{1}^{2}=-A_{2}^{1}
. In particular,
V^{*}
is spanned over
\mathbb{R}
by the real and imaginary parts of

\eta_{j}=dz_{j}+z_{1}A_{1}^{j}+z_{2}A_{2}^{j}
,
j=1,2
. Using that
I\eta_{j}=i\eta_{j}
and
I\bar{\eta}_{j}=-i\bar{\eta}_{j}
we find,

I[d\log(r^{2})]_{1,0}=\frac{i}{|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}}\left({\bar{z}_{1}\eta_{1}-z_{1}\bar{\eta}_{1}+\bar{z}_{2}\eta_{2}-z_{2}\bar{\eta}_{2}}\right).

A trivialization of
S\to Q
on
U_{Q}=\{\ell_{x},\,x\in U,\,v_{1}(x)\not\perp\ell_{x}\}\subset Q
is given by
(x,[1:z],\lambda)\mapsto([v_{1}(x)+zv_{2}(x)],\lambda(v_{1}(x)+zv_{2}(x))
. Its connection
1
-form is

\alpha=\frac{1}{1+|z|^{2}}\left(\pi^{*}(A_{1}^{1})+z\pi^{*}(A_{2}^{1})+\bar{z}(dz+\pi^{*}(A_{1}^{2})+z\pi^{*}(A_{2}^{2})\right).

The curvature
R=d\alpha
takes values in
i\mathbb{R}
, as both
\alpha-\frac{\bar{z}dz}{1+|z|^{2}}
and
d\left(\frac{\bar{z}dz}{1+|z|^{2}}\right)
do. Denote
\pi_{2}\colon S\to\nu(L)
; on
S-Q
we have
\pi_{2}^{*}(dI[d\log(r^{2})]_{1,0})=2\pi_{1}^{*}d\alpha
because:

\pi_{2}^{*}(I[d\log(r^{2})]_{1,0})=\frac{id\lambda}{\lambda}-\frac{id{\bar{\lambda}}}{\bar{\lambda}}-\frac{2}{1+|z|^{2}}\mathrm{Im}\left(\pi^{*}(A_{1}^{1})+z\pi^{*}(A_{2}^{1})+\bar{z}(dz+\pi^{*}(A_{1}^{2})+z\pi^{*}(A_{2}^{2})\right).

Hence,
\pi_{2}^{*}(dI[d\log(r^{2})]_{1,0})
extends as
2i\pi_{1}^{*}(R)
, and the same holds for
dI[d\log(r^{2})]_{1,0}
on
P=S/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

For convenience, consider the closed form
\widehat{\beta}=\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge\widehat{\omega}_{1}+\chi^{*}(\beta_{2})
. With this notation,
\Phi_{1}=\mathrm{pr}^{*}(e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\wedge e_{3})+[\widehat{\beta}]_{2,1}
.
In
[
29
, Section 3.4]
, we considered the parametric family of
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structures on
P
given by

F_{t}^{*}\Phi_{1}=\mathrm{pr}^{*}(e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\wedge e_{3})+t^{2}[\widehat{\beta}]_{2,1}
; its associated metric is
h_{t}=F_{t}^{*}(h_{1})=h_{0,2}^{0}+t^{2}h_{2,0}^{2}
. Hence,
\|\gamma\|_{h_{t}}=\frac{1}{t^{3}}\|[\gamma]_{3,0}\|_{h_{1}}+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\|[\gamma]_{2,1}\|_{h_{1}}+\frac{1}{t}\|[\gamma]_{1,2}\|_{h_{1}}+\|[\gamma]_{0,3}\|_{h_{1}}
if
\gamma\in\Omega^{3}(P)
. In loc. cit., we also obtained
a family of closed
\iota
-invariant forms:

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}_{2}^{t}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}^{*}(e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\wedge e_{3})+t^{2}\widehat{\beta}.

Note that
\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\Phi}^{t}_{2}\in W_{2,1}\oplus W_{1,2}\oplus W_{0,3}
, and we have
[\widehat{\beta}]_{2,1}=[\Phi_{1}]_{2,1}
. In Proposition
36
, we interpolate
\tilde{\mu}(\widehat{\Phi}^{t}_{2})
with
\chi^{*}(\widehat{\phi}^{t}_{3,s})
on
\chi^{-1}(\nu_{2s}(L)/\iota)
. To show that this is a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form, we need the following result, which is analogous to Lemma
31
.

Lemma 35
.

The following equality holds:

[\tilde{\mu}(\widehat{\beta})]_{2,1}=[\widehat{\beta}]_{2,1}.

(40)

Proof.

We prove
[\tilde{\mu}(\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge\widehat{\omega}_{1})]_{2,1}=[\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge\widehat{\omega}_{1}]_{2,1}
and
[\tilde{\mu}(\chi^{*}\beta_{2})]_{2,1}=[\chi^{*}\beta_{2}]_{2,1}
.
By continuity, it suffices to prove the equality on
P-Q
. In addition, since
\tilde{\kappa}
lifts
\kappa
and the splittings
TP=V^{\prime}\oplus H^{\prime}
and
T\nu(L)=V\oplus H
are compatible, it suffices to check a similar equality for the pullback by
\chi^{-1}
of the forms above on
\nu(L)-Z
, replacing
\tilde{\mu}
with
\mu
.
To check the second identity we note that
(\chi^{-1})^{*}(\chi^{*}\beta_{2})=\beta_{2}
; Lemma
31
ensures
[\mu(\beta_{2})]_{2,1}=[\beta_{2}]_{2,1}
. To prove the first, we observe

(\chi^{-1})^{*}(\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge\widehat{\omega}_{1})=-\frac{1}{4}\pi^{*}\theta\wedge d((\|\theta\|^{-1}\circ\pi)I[d\mathsf{f}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}]_{1,0}).

Since

d(\|\theta\|\circ\pi)\in W_{0,1}
we have

[(\chi^{-1})^{*}(\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge\widehat{\omega}_{1})]_{2,1}=-\frac{1}{4}\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge[dI[d\mathsf{f}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}]_{1,0}]_{2,0}=-\frac{1}{4}[\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[d\mathsf{f}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}]_{1,0})]_{2,1}
. The function
\mathsf{f}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}(r)
is
\kappa
-invariant because both the functions
\|\theta\|
and
r
are. Taking Lemmas
28
and
29
into account we get:

\displaystyle[\mu((\chi^{-1})^{*}(\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge\widehat{\omega}_{1}))]_{2,1}=

\displaystyle[\mu([(\chi^{-1})^{*}(\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge\widehat{\omega}_{1})]_{2,1})]_{2,1}=-\frac{1}{4}[\mu([\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[d\mathsf{f}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}]_{1,0})]_{2,1})]_{2,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}[\mu(\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[d\mathsf{f}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}]_{1,0})]_{2,1}=-\frac{1}{4}\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge[dI[d\mathsf{f}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}]_{1,0}]_{2,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\chi^{-1})^{*}(\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge\widehat{\omega}_{1})]_{2,1}.

∎

Proposition 36
.

There is
s_{0}>1
, such that for every
s>s_{0}
there exists
t_{s}^{\prime}
with the property that for all
t<t_{s}^{\prime}
there is a
\tilde{\kappa}
-invariant closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure
\Phi_{3,s}^{t}
on
\chi^{-1}(\nu_{2s}(L))
satisfying
\Phi_{3,s}^{t}=\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})
on
r\leq\frac{s}{8}
and
\Phi_{3,s}^{t}=\chi^{*}(\widehat{\phi}_{3,s}^{t})
on
r\geq\frac{s}{4}
.

Proof.

On the annulus
\frac{s}{8}<r<\frac{s}{4}
we have:

\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})-\chi^{*}(\widehat{\phi}_{3,s}^{t})=\frac{1}{4}t^{2}\tilde{\mu}(d(\mathrm{pr}^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[d\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{|\theta|}(r)]_{1,0})),

where
\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{a}(x)=\mathsf{f}_{a}(x)-x^{2}=\frac{a^{2}}{(x^{4}+a^{2})^{1/2}+x}-a\log((x^{4}+a^{2})^{1/2}+a)+2a\log(x)
. Consider a smooth function
\varpi
such that
\varpi=0
if
x\leq\frac{1}{8}
and
\varpi=1
if
x\geq\frac{1}{4}
and
\varpi_{s}(x)=\varpi(\frac{x}{s})
; then
|\varpi_{s}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{C}{s}
. Define

\xi_{s}=\varpi_{s}\mathrm{pr}^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[d\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{|\theta|}(r)]_{1,0}.

In the proof of
[
29
, Proposition 3.10]
we obtained:

\displaystyle\|[d\xi_{s}]_{2,1}\|_{h_{1}}=

\displaystyle\|\varpi_{s}\mathrm{pr}^{*}e_{1}\wedge[dI[d\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{|\theta|}(r)]_{1,0}]_{2,0}+[d\varpi_{s}]_{1,0}\wedge\mathrm{pr}^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[d\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{|\theta|}(r)]_{1,0}\|_{h_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(r^{-2})+O(r^{-1}s^{-1}).

We now check
[\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})]_{2,1}=[d\xi_{s}]_{2,1}
. As argued in the proof of equation (
40
), it suffices to prove a similar equality for
(\chi^{-1})^{*}d\xi_{s}
, namely
[\mu((\chi^{-1})^{*}d\xi_{s})]_{2,1}=[(\chi^{-1})^{*}d\xi_{s}]_{2,1}
, on
(\nu(L)-Z)/\iota
.
Since
\kappa^{*}\varpi_{s}=\varpi_{s}
,

\displaystyle\mu((\chi^{-1})^{*}d\xi_{s})=

\displaystyle d\varpi_{s}\wedge\mu(\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[d\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}(r)]_{1,0})+\varpi_{s}\mu(\pi^{*}de_{1}\wedge I[d\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}(r)]_{1,0})

\displaystyle+\varpi_{s}\mu(\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge dI[d\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}(r)]_{1,0}).

Since
\mathsf{f}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}
is
\kappa
-invariant, Lemmas
28
and
29
allow us to conclude:

[\mu((\chi^{-1})^{*}d\xi_{s})]_{2,1}=[d\varpi_{s}]_{1,0}\wedge\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge I[d\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}(r)]_{1,0}+\varpi_{s}\pi^{*}e_{1}\wedge[dI[d\bar{\mathsf{f}}_{\|\theta\|\circ\pi}(r)]_{1,0}]_{2,0}=[d((\chi^{-1})^{*}\xi_{s})]_{2,1}.

Therefore,

\|[\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})]_{2,1}\|_{h_{1}}=\|[d\xi_{s}]_{2,1}\|_{h_{1}}=O(r^{-2})+O(r^{-1}s^{-1})
.

We set
s_{0}>1
such that for every
s>s_{0}
we have

|O(r^{-2})+O(r^{-1}s^{-1})|<m

on
\frac{s}{8}\leq r\leq\frac{s}{4}
. Here,
m
is the constant from Lemma
12
. Given
s>s_{0}
, we can find
t_{s}^{\prime}<t_{s}
such that on
\frac{s}{8}\leq r\leq\frac{s}{4}
we have,

t\|[\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})]_{1,1}\|_{h_{1}}<m,\qquad t^{2}\|[\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})]_{0,2}\|_{h_{1}}<m.

Fixed
s>0
, using equation (
40
), and the fact that
[\tilde{\mu}(\widehat{\beta})]_{3,0}=0
, we obtain

\|\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})-\Phi_{1}^{t}\|_{h_{t}}=t\|[\tilde{\mu}(\widehat{\beta})]_{1,2}\|_{h_{1}}+t^{2}\|[\tilde{\mu}(\widehat{\beta})]_{0,3}\|_{h_{1}}.

Hence, we can assume that on
\nu_{2s}(L)
we have

\|\mu(\Phi_{2}^{t})-\Phi_{1}^{t}\|_{h_{t}}<\frac{m}{4}

for
t<t_{s}^{\prime}
. In particular,
\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})
is a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form on
\nu_{2s}(L)
. The closed form

\Phi_{3,s}^{t}=\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})-\frac{t^{2}}{4}\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s}),

coincides with
\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})
on
r\leq\frac{s}{8}
, and with
\chi^{*}(\widehat{\phi}_{3,s}^{t})
on
r\geq\frac{s}{4}
. Hence,
\Phi_{3,s}^{t}
is a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form outside the neck
\frac{s}{8}\leq r\leq\frac{s}{4}
. There, we have that:

\|\Phi_{3,s}^{t}-\Phi_{1}^{t}\|_{h_{t}}\leq\|\Phi_{3,s}^{t}-\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})\|_{h_{t}}+\|\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})-\Phi_{1}^{t}\|_{h_{t}}<\|\Phi_{3,s}^{t}-\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})\|_{h_{t}}+\frac{m}{4}.

The fact that
\Phi_{3,s}^{t}
is a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form also on
\frac{s}{8}\leq r\leq\frac{s}{4}
follows from Lemma
12
and the following inequality:

\displaystyle 4\|\Phi_{3,s}^{t}-\tilde{\mu}(\Phi_{2}^{t})\|_{h_{t}}

\displaystyle=t^{2}\|\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})\|_{h_{t}}=\|[\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})]_{2,1}\|_{h_{1}}+t\|[\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})]_{1,2}\|_{h_{1}}+t^{2}\|[\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})]_{0,3}\|_{h_{1}}

\displaystyle=O(r^{-2})+O(r^{-1}s^{-1})+t\|[\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})]_{1,2}\|_{h_{1}}+t^{2}\|[\tilde{\mu}(d\xi_{s})]_{0,3}\|_{h_{1}}<3m.

∎

Finally, we glue an annulus around
Z
on
(\nu(L)/\iota,\phi_{2})
with an annulus around
Q
on
(P,{\Phi}_{3,s}^{t})
using the map
F_{t}\circ\chi
.

Theorem 37
.

Let
\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{R}{4}
and
s_{0}>0
be the values provided by Propositions
27
and

36
.
Let
s>s_{0}
, and let
D_{s}(Q)
be the disc in
P
centered at
Q
with radius
s
. If
t<\min\{t_{s}^{\prime},\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{s}\}
, then there is a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
resolution
\rho\colon(\widetilde{X}_{t},\tilde{\varphi}_{t},\widetilde{g})\to({X},{\varphi},{g})
, of the form

\widetilde{X}_{t}=X-\exp(\nu_{\varepsilon}(L)/\iota)\cup_{\exp\circ F_{t}\circ\chi}D_{s}(Q),

(41)

for
\varepsilon=ts>0
. In addition, there are finite-order diffeomorphisms
\tilde{\kappa}_{t}\colon\widetilde{X}_{t}\to\widetilde{X}_{t}
that lift
\kappa
and such that
\tilde{\kappa}_{t}^{*}(\tilde{\varphi_{t}})=\tilde{\varphi}_{t}
.

Proof.

Given such values for
s
and
t
, we define
\varepsilon=st\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. The map
F_{t}\circ\chi
identifies
\frac{s}{2}\leq r\leq 2s
on
P
with
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\leq r\leq 2\varepsilon
on
\nu(L)/\iota
. In terms of equation (
41
), the projection
\rho\colon\widetilde{X}_{t}\to X
is defined by
\rho|_{X}=\mathrm{Id}
and
\rho|_{D_{s}(Q)}=\exp\circ F_{t}\circ\chi
.
The lift of
\kappa
on
D_{s}(Q)
is determined by
\tilde{\kappa}
, it is well-defined because
\kappa
commutes with both
F_{t}
and
\exp
.

Consider
\tilde{\kappa}
-invariant
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form
\Phi_{3,s}^{t}
on
\chi^{-1}(\nu(L)_{2s}/\iota)
and the
\kappa
-invariant form
\phi_{3,\varepsilon}
on
X
. On the region
\frac{s}{2}\leq r\leq 2s
of
\chi^{-1}(\nu_{2s}(L)/\iota)
we have that
\Phi_{3,s}^{t}=\chi^{*}(\widehat{\phi}_{3,s}^{t})=\phi_{2}^{t}
, and on the annulus
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\leq r\leq 2\varepsilon
of
\nu(L)/\iota
we have
\phi_{3,\varepsilon}=\phi_{2}
. Since
(F_{t}\circ\chi)^{*}\phi_{2}=\chi^{*}(\phi_{2}^{t})
, there is a
\tilde{\kappa}
-invariant
\mathrm{G}_{2}
form
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}\in\Omega^{3}(\widetilde{X}_{t})
.
∎

Remark 13
.

The manifolds
\widetilde{X}_{t}
are canonically diffeomorphic. Hence, we denote them by
\widetilde{X}
.

4.5
Cohomology of
\widetilde{X}/\tilde{\kappa}

Proposition
19
allows us to compute
H^{*}(\widetilde{X})
, assuming that at each
L_{j}
, there is a projection
\mathrm{q}_{j}\colon L_{j}\to S^{1}
such that
\theta|_{L_{j}}=\mathrm{q}_{j}^{*}(dt)
. To determine the
\tilde{\kappa}
-invariant part, we first study
the action of
\tilde{\kappa}
on the Thom classes
[\tau_{j}]
of
Q_{j}=Q|_{L_{j}}
.

Lemma 38
.

If
\kappa(L_{j})=L_{k}
, then

\sigma|_{L_{j}}\tilde{\kappa}^{*}(\tau_{k})
is a Thom form of
Q_{j}
.

Proof.

Let
x=[F_{p},[0,\ell]]\in Q
. The fiber of the normal bundle of
Q\subset P
at
x
is
the tangent space of
\{[F_{p},[v,\ell]],\quad v\in\mathbb{C}\}
at
v=0
and it is oriented by
\frac{i}{2}dv\wedge d\bar{v}
.
From our description, we see that if
\sigma|_{L_{j}}=1
, then
\tilde{\kappa}[G_{p},[v,\ell]]=[G_{p}\circ(\kappa|_{p})^{-1},[v,\ell]]
. Therefore,
\tilde{\kappa}
preserves the orientation of the fibers of the normal bundle.
However, if
\sigma|_{L_{j}}=-1
then
\tilde{\kappa}[G_{p},[v,\ell]]=[c(G_{p})\circ(\kappa|_{p})^{-1},\overline{[{v},{\ell}]}]
, so that
\tilde{\kappa}
reverses the orientation of the fibers. Hence
\tilde{\kappa}^{*}\tau_{k}
integrates to
\sigma|_{L_{j}}
over the fibers of the normal neighborhood of
L_{j}
, and is supported around
Q_{j}
. Hence,
\sigma|_{L_{j}}\tilde{\kappa}^{*}\tau_{k}
is a Thom form of
Q_{j}
.
∎

Lemma 39
.

In terms of the splitting in Proposition
19
,

[\tilde{\varphi}_{t}]=[\varphi]-\pi t^{2}\sum_{j}[\pi^{*}\theta]\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}.

Proof.

Using that
\beta_{2}
and
\pi^{*}\theta\wedge d(|\theta|^{-1}I[d\mathbf{g}_{|\theta|}]_{1,0})
are exact we obtain:

[\tilde{\varphi}_{t}]|_{Q_{j}}=[\tilde{\mu}{(\Phi_{2}^{t})}]|_{Q_{j}}=[\mathrm{pr}^{*}(\varphi|_{L_{j}})+t^{2}\tilde{\mu}(\mathrm{pr}^{*}\theta\wedge\widehat{\omega}_{1})]|_{Q_{j}}=[\mathrm{pr}^{*}(\varphi)-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\tilde{\mu}(\pi^{*}\theta\wedge d(I[d\log(r^{2})]_{1,0})]|_{Q_{j}}.

Let
R_{j}
be the curvature of the tautological line bundle
S_{j}\to Q_{j}
. Notations as in Proposition
16
,
from Remarks
5
and
12
it follows
[\tau_{j}]|_{Q_{j}}=2\mathrm{e}_{j}=\frac{i}{\pi}[R_{j}]=\frac{1}{2\pi}d(I[d\log(r^{2})]_{1,0})])|_{Q_{j}}
.
Lemma
38
shows

[\tilde{\varphi}_{t}]|_{Q_{j}}=[\mathrm{pr}^{*}(\varphi)-\pi t^{2}\pi^{*}\theta\wedge\tau_{j}]|_{Q_{j}}.

Proposition
17
implies that
[\tilde{\varphi}_{t}+\pi t^{2}\sum_{j}\pi^{*}\theta\wedge\tau_{j}]\in\mathrm{Im}(\rho^{*})
. We finally prove that
\xi=[\tilde{\varphi}_{t}+\pi t^{2}\sum_{j}\pi^{*}\theta\wedge\tau_{j}-\rho^{*}(\varphi)]
vanishes on
H^{*}(\widetilde{X})
. Since
\xi\in\mathrm{Im}(\rho^{*})
, it suffices to check that
\xi\in\oplus_{j}\mathrm{pr}^{*}(H^{1}(L_{j}))\wedge[\tau_{j}]
by Proposition
17
. Using that

[\exp_{*}(\phi_{2})]|_{X-L}=[\varphi]|_{X-L}
, we obtain
\xi|_{\widetilde{X}-D_{s}(Q)}=[\tilde{\varphi}_{t}-\rho^{*}(\varphi)]_{\widetilde{X}-D_{s}(Q)}=0
. The long exact sequence of the pair
(X,X-D_{s}(Q))
shows
\xi\in H^{3}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{X}-D_{s}(Q))
.
Since
\widetilde{X}-Q
retracts onto
\widetilde{X}-D_{s}(Q)
, Lemma
21
ensures

\xi\in\oplus_{j}\mathrm{pr}^{*}(H^{1}(L_{j}))\wedge[\tau_{j}]
.
∎

From Propositions
19
and
26
, using that
L_{j}
are
\varphi
-calibrated submanifolds, and the orientations discussed at the end of section
2.4
, it follows

\int_{\widetilde{X}}{p_{1}(\tilde{X})\wedge\widetilde{\varphi}_{t}}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}{p_{1}(M)\wedge\varphi}-3\sum_{j}\mathsf{vol}(L_{j})+8t^{2}\pi\sum_{j}(1-g_{j})\int_{L_{j}}\theta\wedge\omega_{j},\quad\int_{L_{j}}\theta\wedge\omega_{j}>0.

(42)

For the remainder of the section, we additionally assume:

1.

The map
\kappa
is an involution.

2.

If
\kappa(L_{j})=L_{k}\neq L_{j}
, then
\theta|_{L_{k}}=\kappa^{*}(\theta|_{L_{k}})
. Hence, by (1),
\sigma|_{L_{j}}=\sigma|_{L_{k}}=1
.

We divide the set of connected components of
L
into two subsets. Define
\mathcal{L}_{F}^{\pm}
as the set of connected components
L_{j}
fixed by
\kappa
such that
\sigma|_{L_{j}}=\pm 1
, and
\mathcal{L}_{S}
the set of connected components that are not fixed by
\kappa
. As
\kappa
is an involution, there are indices
j_{1},\dots,j_{r}
such that
\mathcal{L}_{S}=\{L_{j_{s}}\}_{s=1}^{r}\sqcup\{\kappa(L_{j_{s}})\}_{s=1}^{r}
.
We also denote
H^{*}(L_{j})^{\pm}=\{[\beta]\in H^{*}(L_{j})\colon\kappa^{*}[\beta]=\pm[\beta]\}
if
L_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{F}^{\pm}
.

Proposition 40
.

In the notations of Proposition
19
,
there is an isomorphism between
H^{*}(\widetilde{X})^{\tilde{\kappa}}
and

\displaystyle H^{*}(M)^{\langle\iota,\kappa\rangle}\oplus\left(\oplus_{s=1}^{r}(\Id+\kappa)^{*}(H^{*-2}(L_{j_{s}})\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j_{s}})\right)

\displaystyle\oplus\left(\oplus_{L_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{F}^{+}}H^{*-2}(L_{j})^{+}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}\right)\oplus\left(\oplus_{L_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{F}^{-}}H^{*-2}(L_{j})^{-}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}\right),

where we defined
\kappa^{*}(\mathbf{x}_{j}):=\mathbf{x}_{k}

if
L_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
and
\kappa(L_{j})=L_{k}
.

Proof.

We first note that
\tilde{\kappa}
acts on the subspaces
H^{*}(X)
and
\oplus_{j}H^{*-2}(L_{j})\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}
. It restricts to
\kappa^{*}
on the first of these, and as
\kappa^{*}\otimes\kappa^{*}
in the second, where we denote
\kappa^{*}(\mathbf{x}_{j})=\sigma|_{L_{j}}\mathbf{x}_{k}
if
\kappa(L_{j})=L_{k}
. This notation is consistent with Lemma
38
. An average argument yields
H^{*}(X)^{\kappa}=H^{*}(M)^{\langle\iota,\kappa\rangle}
. In
addition, if
L_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{F}^{\pm}
then
H^{*-2}(L_{j})\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}
is
(\kappa^{*}\otimes\kappa^{*})
-invariant; its fixed part is
H^{*-2}(L_{j})^{\pm}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}
when
L_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{F}^{\pm}
. Finally, if
L_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
, then
H^{*}(L_{j})\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}\oplus H^{*}(\kappa(L_{j}))\otimes\kappa^{*}(\mathbf{x}_{j})
is
(\kappa^{*}\otimes\kappa^{*})
-invariant, and its fixed part is
(\Id+\kappa)^{*}(H^{*}(L_{j_{s}})\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j_{s}})
.
∎

Remark 14
.

Let
K=\operatorname{Fix}(\tilde{\kappa})
, and denote by
K_{j}
its connected components. Assume that these are the mapping torus of a certain volume-preserving diffeomorphism
f_{j}\colon\Sigma_{j}\to\Sigma_{j}
, where
\Sigma_{j}
is a surface of genus
g_{j}
. Then, there exists a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
resolution

Z
of
\widetilde{X}/\tilde{\kappa}
. Its cohomology algebra is isomorphic to

\displaystyle H^{*}(\widetilde{X})^{\tilde{\kappa}}\oplus(\oplus_{j}H^{*}(K_{j})\otimes\mathbf{y}_{j}),

Now
\mathbf{y}_{j}
represents the Thom class of
\rho^{-1}(K_{j})
on the resolution.
Let
[\omega_{j}]\in H^{2}(K_{j})
be the
the cohomology class
determined by the volume form of
\Sigma_{j}
that integrates to
1
, then

\mathbf{y}_{j}^{2}=-2PD[K_{j}]+(4-4g_{p})[\omega_{j}]\otimes\mathbf{y}_{j}
. In addition, every
L_{j_{s}}\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
, is disjoint from
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
in
M
. Therefore, the connected components of the exceptional divisor of
\widetilde{X}
corresponding to
\cup L_{j_{s}}
are disjoint from
\operatorname{Fix}(\tilde{\kappa})
, and we can assume
(\Id+\kappa)^{*}(\mathbf{x}_{j_{s}})\cdot\mathbf{y}_{j}=0
.

5
Compact manifolds with a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure satisfying the known topological properties of compact holonomy
\mathrm{G}_{2}
manifolds

5.1
Set-up

Let
N
be a
6
-dimensional manifold, let
F\in\Diff(N)
, and set
M=N_{F}
be the mapping torus of
F
, defined as in expression (
2
).
Suppose that there are both a path
\{(\omega_{t},J_{t})\}_{t\in[0,1]}
of Hermitian structures on
N
, and a path
\{\Theta_{t}=\psi^{+}_{t}+i\psi_{t}^{-}\}
of
J_{t}
-complex volume forms. If
F^{*}(\omega_{1})=\omega_{0}
, and
F^{*}(\psi_{1}^{+})=\psi_{0}^{+}
, then

\varphi=dt\wedge\omega_{t}+\psi_{t}^{+},

determines a
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure on
N_{F}
.
It is closed if

d\omega_{t}=-\dot{\psi}_{t}^{+},\mbox{ and }d\psi_{t}^{+}=0.

(43)

Consider an involution
\eta\colon N\to N
that satisfies
\eta\circ F=F^{-1}\circ\eta
. The map
\kappa\colon N_{F}\to N_{F}
,
\kappa[t,p]=[1-t,\eta(p)]
preserves
\varphi
if

\eta^{*}(\omega_{t})=-\omega_{1-t},\mbox{ and }\eta^{*}(\psi^{+}_{t})=\psi^{+}_{1-t}.

(44)

If there is also an involution
\xi\colon N\to N
satisfying
\xi\circ F=F\circ\xi
. The map
\iota\colon N_{F}\to N_{F}
,
\iota[t,p]=[t,\xi(p)]
preserves
\varphi
if
\xi
preserves both the paths
\{\omega_{t}\}
and
\{\psi_{t}^{+}\}
. We assume
\xi\circ\eta=\eta\circ\xi
so that the group generated by
\iota
and
\kappa
is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

In this situation, we have closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
orbifolds
(X^{1}=N_{F}/\kappa,\varphi)
and
(X^{2}=N_{F}/\langle\iota,\kappa\rangle,\varphi)
. The singular locus of
X^{1}
and
X^{2}
are respectively
\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)
, and
\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)\cup\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)\cup\operatorname{Fix}(\iota\circ\kappa)
. Equations (
3
) and (
4
) allow us to compute these. Further assumptions allow us to resolve the orbifolds
X^{1}
and
X^{2}
using
[
29
, Theorem 3.11]
and Theorem
37
.

5.2
Examples

Our examples are constructed from three different complex
6
-tori that we now describe. Consider the lattices of
\mathbb{C}

\Gamma_{1}=\mathbb{Z}\langle 1,e^{\frac{2\pi i}{3}}\rangle,\qquad\Gamma_{2}=\mathbb{Z}\langle 1,i\rangle,\qquad\Gamma_{3}=\mathbb{Z}\langle 1+i,1-i\rangle\subset\Gamma_{2},

and the tori

T^{6}_{k}=(\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})^{3},\qquad k=1,2,\qquad T^{6}_{3}=\mathbb{C}^{3}/(\Gamma_{3}\times\Gamma_{2}^{2}).

We proceed to define diffeomorphisms
F_{k,a}\colon T^{6}_{k}\to T^{6}_{k}
for
(k,a)\in\{(1,3),(1,6),(2,4),(3,4)\}
,
as well as paths
\{(\omega_{t},J_{t})\}
, and
\{\Theta_{t}\}
and involutions
\eta_{k},\xi_{k}\colon T^{6}_{k}\to T^{6}_{k}
, for
k=1,2,3
and
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\colon T^{6}_{k}\to T^{6}_{k}
for
k=2,3
. In the sequel, we denote points on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
by
z=(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})
, and points on
T^{6}_{k}
by
[z]
.

1.

The mapping diffeomorphisms
F_{k,a}
are the composition of a complex rotation
\mathrm{R}_{a}
on
T^{6}_{k}
and a diffeomorphism
f
, which we now define. Given
a\in\mathbb{R}
, we let

\mathrm{R}_{a}\colon\mathbb{C}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}^{3},\quad\mathrm{R}_{a}(z)=(e^{\frac{2\pi i}{a}}z_{1},e^{\frac{2\pi i}{a}}z_{2},e^{-\frac{4\pi i}{a}}z_{3}).

For the moment, we only need to take
a=3,4,6
.
Observe that
\mathrm{R}_{3}
and
\mathrm{R}_{6}
descend to
T^{6}_{1}
while
\mathrm{R}_{4}
descends to
T^{6}_{2}
and
T^{6}_{3}
. Let

f\colon\mathbb{C}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}^{3},\quad f(z)=(z_{1},z_{2}-z_{1},z_{3}),

and note it descends to
T^{6}_{k}
for
k=1,2,3
, and it
commutes with
\mathrm{R}_{a}
. The diffeomorphisms are:

\displaystyle F_{1,a}\colon T^{6}_{1}\to T^{6}_{1},\quad F_{1,a}=f\circ\mathrm{R}_{a},\qquad a\in\{3,6\},

\displaystyle F_{k,4}\colon T^{6}_{k}\to T^{6}_{k},\quad F_{2,a}=f\circ\mathrm{R}_{4},\qquad k\in\{2,3\}.

2.

The paths
\{J_{t}\}
and
\{\Theta_{t}\}
are constant and determined by the standard complex structure on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
and the standard volume form respectively. The volume form is
F_{k,a}
-invariant. We define path of non-degenerate
(1,1)
-forms
\omega_{t}
:

\omega_{t}=\frac{i}{2}\left((1+t^{2})dz_{1\bar{1}}+dz_{2\bar{2}}+dz_{3\bar{3}}+t\,(dz_{1\bar{2}}-dz_{\bar{1}2})\right).

(45)

The equality
F_{k,a}^{*}(\omega_{1})=\omega_{0}
follows from

\mathrm{R}_{a}^{*}(\omega_{t})=\omega_{t}
and
f^{*}(\omega_{1})=\omega_{0}
, the latter is obtained from
f^{*}(dz_{2\bar{2}})=dz_{1\bar{1}}-(dz_{1\bar{2}}-dz_{\bar{1}2})+dz_{2\bar{2}}
.

3.

Consider the involutions
\xi,\eta\colon\mathbb{C}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}^{3}
,

\displaystyle\xi(z)=

\displaystyle(-z_{1},-z_{2},z_{3}),

\displaystyle\eta(z)=

\displaystyle(-\overline{z}_{1},\overline{z}_{2}+\overline{z}_{1},-\overline{z}_{3}).

Denote by
\xi_{k},\eta_{k}\colon T^{6}_{k}\to T^{6}_{k}
the induced maps. It is clear that
\xi_{k}
satisfies all the assumptions discussed in section
5.1
, and that
\xi_{k}\circ\eta_{k}=\eta_{k}\circ\xi_{k}
. Concerning
\eta_{k}
, we
observe that
\eta\circ\mathrm{R}_{a}=\mathrm{R}_{a}^{-1}\circ\eta
, and

\eta\circ f=(-\overline{z}_{1},\overline{z}_{2},-\overline{z}_{3})
is an involution; therefore,
f_{k}\circ\eta=f_{k}^{-1}\circ\eta
.
These equalities yield
\eta_{k}\circ F_{k,a}=F_{k,a}^{-1}\circ\eta_{k}
. To check that
\eta_{k}
satisfies the conditions in equation (
44
), we first observe that
\eta_{k}^{*}(\psi_{t}^{+})=\psi_{t}^{+}
because
\eta^{*}(dz_{123})=dz_{\bar{1}\bar{2}\bar{3}}
on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
. In addition, the equality
\eta_{k}^{*}(\omega_{t})=-\omega_{1-t}
follows from
\eta^{*}(dz_{p\bar{p}})=-dz_{p\bar{p}}
for
p\neq 2
, as well as the computations

\displaystyle\eta^{*}(dz_{2\bar{2}})=

\displaystyle-dz_{1\bar{1}}+dz_{1\bar{2}}-dz_{\bar{1}2}-dz_{2\bar{2}},

(46)

\displaystyle\eta^{*}(dz_{1\bar{2}}-dz_{\bar{1}2})=

\displaystyle-2dz_{1\bar{1}}-dz_{1\bar{2}}+dz_{\bar{1}2}.

(47)

4.

Consider the maps
\zeta^{1},\zeta^{2}\colon\mathbb{C}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}^{3}
,

\zeta^{1}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})=(-z_{1},-z_{2},z_{3}+1/2),\qquad\zeta^{2}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})=(-z_{1},-z_{2},z_{3}+i/2).

For
k=2,3
, these maps descend to involutions
\zeta^{1}_{k},\zeta^{2}_{k}\colon T^{6}_{k}\to T^{6}_{k}
, which commute with both
F_{k,4}
and
\eta_{k}
. They preserve the paths
\{\omega_{t}\}
and
\{\psi_{t}^{+}\}
.

5.

If
k=1,2
, the fixed points of the involutions
\eta_{k}
are determined by the equations:

z_{1}\equiv-2i\mathrm{Im}(z_{2})\mod\Gamma_{k},\qquad 2\mathrm{Re}(z_{3})\equiv 0\mod\Gamma_{k}.

(48)

If
k=3
, we have the same equations as for
k=2
. To check that in this case,
z_{1}\equiv-\overline{z}_{1}\mod\Gamma_{3}
one observes that
z_{1}+\overline{z}_{1}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\subset\Gamma_{3}
. Similarly, when
k=1,2
, a fixed point
[z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}]
of
\eta_{k}\circ\xi_{k}
satisfies
z_{1}\equiv-2\mathrm{Re}(z_{2})\mod\Gamma_{k}
, and
2\mathrm{Re}(z_{3})\equiv 0\mod\Gamma_{k}
. For
k=3
, the equations are again the same as for
k=2
.

6.

When
k=2,3
we will direcly compute
\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{2}\circ f\circ\mathrm{R}_{4})
and
\operatorname{Fix}(\xi_{2}\circ\eta_{2}\circ f\circ\mathrm{R}_{4})
. However, we will rely on the following observation for
k=1
.
Given
a=3,6
, using the equalities
\mathrm{R}_{a}=\mathrm{R}_{2a}^{2}
, and
\mathrm{R}_{2a}^{-1}\circ\eta=\eta\circ\mathrm{R}_{2a}
, we obtain
\eta_{1}\circ f\circ\mathrm{R}_{a}=\mathrm{R}_{2a}^{-1}\circ\eta_{1}\circ f\circ\mathrm{R}_{2a}
. Hence,

[z]\in\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{1}\circ f\circ\mathrm{R}_{a})\quad\mbox{ if and only if }\quad(\eta_{1}\circ f)(\mathrm{R}_{2a}(z))\equiv R_{2a}(z)\mod R_{2a}(\Gamma_{1}^{3}).

(49)

In addition,
\eta_{1}\circ f[z]=[-\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{2},-\bar{z}_{3}]
. Hence, to determine
\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{1}\circ f\circ\mathrm{R}_{a})
it suffices to solve

\eta_{1}\circ f(z)\equiv z\mod\mathrm{R}_{2a}(\Gamma_{1}^{3})

and then compute
\mathrm{R}_{-2a}(z)
. The solutions to the congruence equation are
z\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
that satisfy

2\,(\mathrm{Re}(z_{1}),i\mathrm{Im}(z_{2}),\mathrm{Re}(z_{3}))\in\mathrm{R}_{2a}(\Gamma_{1}^{3}).

Similarly,
[z]\in\operatorname{Fix}(\xi_{1}\circ\eta_{1}\circ f\circ\mathrm{R}_{a})
if and only if

(\xi_{1}\circ\eta_{1}\circ f)(\mathrm{R}_{2a}(z))\equiv R_{2a}(z)\mod R_{2a}(\Gamma_{1}^{3})
and

\xi_{1}\circ\eta_{1}\circ f[z]=[\bar{z}_{1},-\bar{z}_{2},-\bar{z}_{3}]
. A similar strategy can be used to compute
\operatorname{Fix}(\xi_{1}\circ\eta_{1}\circ f\circ\mathrm{R}_{a})
, which is given by the classes
[\mathrm{R}_{-2a}(z)]
where
z\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
satisfies

2\,(i\mathrm{Im}(z_{1}),\mathrm{Re}(z_{2}),\mathrm{Re}(z_{3}))\in\mathrm{R}_{2a}(\Gamma_{1}^{3})
.
Note also that
\Gamma_{1}^{3}
is
\mathrm{R}_{6}
-invariant but it is not
\mathrm{R}_{12}
-invariant. Indeed,

\mathrm{R}_{12}(\Gamma_{1}^{3})=(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Z}\langle e^{\pi i/6},e^{5\pi i/6}\rangle)^{2}\times\Gamma_{1}=(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Z}\langle e^{\pi i/6},i\rangle)^{2}\times\Gamma_{1}.

Define
M_{k,a}
the mapping torus of
F_{k,a}
, and denote by
\varphi
the
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure determined by the paths
\{\omega_{t},J_{t}\}
and
\{\Theta_{t}\}
. Let
\iota
and
\kappa
be the involutions of
M_{k,a}
determined by
\xi_{k}
and
\eta_{k}
. In addition, if
k=2,3
we set
\mathrm{j}_{1}
, and
\mathrm{j}_{2}
on
M_{k,4}
the involutions determined by
\zeta^{1}_{k}
and
\zeta^{2}_{k}
. Define the closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
orbifolds:

\displaystyle(X_{k,a}^{1}=M_{k,a}/\kappa,\varphi),\quad

\displaystyle(k,a)\in\{(1,3),(1,6),(2,4),(3,4)\},

\displaystyle(X^{2}_{k,a}=M_{k,a}/\langle\iota,\kappa\rangle,\varphi),\quad

\displaystyle(k,a)\in\{(1,3),(1,6),(2,4)\},

\displaystyle(Z_{k,4}=M_{k,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2},\kappa\rangle,\varphi),\quad

\displaystyle k=2,3.

We introduced the orbifolds
X_{3,4}^{1}
and
Z_{3,4}
because they are the universal cover of
X_{2,4}^{1}
and
Z_{3,4}^{1}
respectively (see the proof of Proposition
45
). Since
X_{3,4}^{2}
is simply connected, we will not deal with
X_{3,4}^{2}
.

Remark 15
.

In
[
9
, Theorem 4]
, they find a left-invariant closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure in the Lie group
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}
endowed with the product
(t,z)\cdot(s,w)=(t+s,z+\rho(t)(w))
, with
\rho(t)=\mathrm{Id}+t(f-\mathrm{Id})=\exp(tf-t\mathrm{Id})
, and
f
defined as above. The Lie algebra of this group is denoted by
(0,0,0,0,12,13,0)
in that result.
If
k=1,2
we set
\Lambda_{k,a}
the subgroup spanned by
a\mathbb{Z}\times\{0\}
and
\{0\}\times\Gamma_{k}^{3}
. If
k=3
, the lattice
\Lambda_{k,a}
is spanned by
a\mathbb{Z}\times\{0\}
and
\{0\}\times\Gamma_{3}\times\Gamma_{2}^{2}
. Then
M_{k,a}^{\prime}=(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{3})/\Lambda_{k,a}
is a nilmanifold with a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure. Note that
M_{k,a}^{\prime}=(\mathbb{R}\times T^{6}_{k})/a\mathbb{Z}
,
where
a\mathbb{Z}
acts as
an\cdot(t,[z])=(t+an,f^{an}[z])
because
\rho(an)(z)=(z_{1},z_{2}-anz_{1},z_{3})=f^{an}(z)
.
In addition, equation (
2
) yields

M_{k,a}=(\mathbb{R}\times T^{6}_{k})/\mathbb{Z}
, with
\mathbb{Z}
acting as
n\cdot(t,z)=(t+n,F_{k,a}^{n}(z))
. Since
F_{k,a}^{a}=f^{a}
, there is a
a:1
covering

M_{k,a}^{\prime}\to M_{k,a},\quad[t,[z]]\to[t,[z]].

The
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure on
M_{k,a}^{\prime}
pushes forward to
M_{k,a}
and coincides with the closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure we described. Another closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
orbifold can be constructed from the nilpotent Lie algebra
(0,0,0,12,13,14,15)
(see loc. cit. Lemma 6).

Remark 16
.

The torsion of the constructed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structures is not small. From

\star\varphi=\frac{1}{2}\omega_{t}\wedge\omega_{t}-dt\wedge\mathrm{Im}(\Theta)
, we deduce

d(\star\varphi)=-\frac{t}{2}dt\wedge dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}}-\frac{t}{2}dt\wedge dz_{1\bar{1}3\bar{3}}-\frac{1}{4}dt\wedge dz_{1\bar{2}3\bar{3}}+\frac{1}{4}dt\wedge dz_{\bar{1}23\bar{3}}
.
In addition, the associated metric is

dt^{2}+(1+t^{2})dx_{1}^{2}+2t\,dx_{1}\cdot dx_{2}+dx_{2}^{2}+(1+t^{2})dy_{1}^{2}+2t\,dy_{1}\cdot dy_{2}+dy_{2}^{2}+dx_{3}^{2}+dy_{3}^{2}.

Remark 17
.

Some orbifolds remain to be studied, whose analysis is analogous to that carried out in the next sections. Obvious cases include
M_{k,a}/\kappa\circ\iota
, for all the pairs
(k,a)
considered, as well as
M_{3,4}/\langle\iota,\kappa\rangle
, and
M_{k,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{i},\kappa\rangle
for
k=2,3
and
i=1,2
. Similar to
X_{3,4}^{1}
, one could also consider the lattice
\Gamma_{4}=\mathbb{Z}\langle 3,1+e^{\pi i/3}\rangle\subset\Gamma_{1}
, and define
M_{4,3}
as the mapping torus of
f\circ R_{3}\colon\mathbb{C}^{3}/(\Gamma_{4}\times\Gamma_{1}^{2})\to\mathbb{C}^{3}/(\Gamma_{4}\times\Gamma_{1}^{2})
. One could then study the orbifolds
M_{4,3}/\kappa
,
M_{4,3}/\iota\circ\kappa
,
M_{4,3}/\langle\iota,\kappa\rangle
.

5.2.1
Singular locus

We first describe the singular locus of
X_{k,a}^{1}
and
X^{2}_{k,a}
for
k=1,2
. As shown by equations (
3
) and (
4
), these loci are determined by
\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{1})
,
\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{1}\circ F_{1,a})
,
\operatorname{Fix}(\xi_{1}\circ\eta_{1})
and
\operatorname{Fix}(\xi_{1}\circ\eta_{1}\circ F_{1,a})
, which we compute using the formulas (
48
), (
49
), and the observations in the paragraphs containing these. If

k=1
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{1})=

\displaystyle\{[-i2y_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},iy_{3}],\quad 0\leq 2y_{2},y_{3}\leq\sqrt{3},\quad-y_{2}/\sqrt{3}\leq x_{2}\leq 1-y_{2}/\sqrt{3}\},

(50)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\xi_{1}\circ\eta_{1})=

\displaystyle\{[-2x_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},iy_{3}],\quad 0\leq 2y_{2},y_{3}\leq\sqrt{3},\quad-y_{2}/\sqrt{3}\leq x_{2}\leq 1-y_{2}/\sqrt{3}\},

(51)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{1}\circ F_{1,a})=

\displaystyle\{[\mathrm{R}_{-2a}(iy_{1},x_{2},iy_{3})],\quad 0\leq y_{1}\leq\mathbf{s}_{a},\quad 0\leq x_{2}\leq\mathbf{t}_{a},\quad 0\leq y_{3}\leq\sqrt{3}\},

(52)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\xi_{1}\circ\eta_{1}\circ F_{1,a})=

\displaystyle\{[\mathrm{R}_{-2a}(x_{1},iy_{2},iy_{3})],\quad 0\leq x_{1}\leq\mathbf{t}_{a},\quad 0\leq y_{2}\leq\mathbf{s}_{a},\quad 0\leq y_{3}\leq\sqrt{3}\},

(53)

where
\mathbf{t}_{3}=\mathbf{s}_{6}=1
,
\mathbf{t}_{6}=\mathbf{s}_{3}=\sqrt{3}
.

From equality (
4
) we obtain that both
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
and
\operatorname{Fix}(\iota\circ\kappa)
consist of two submanifolds diffeomorphic to a torus
T^{3}
. The singular locus of
X_{1,a}^{1}
is
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
; we denote the connected components at
t=0
and
t=1/2
as
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
respectively.

For
k=2
, we have:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{2})=

\displaystyle\{[-2iy_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},\varepsilon_{3}+iy_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{0,1/2\},\quad 0\leq x_{2},y_{2},y_{3}\leq 1\},

(54)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\xi_{2}\circ\eta_{2})=

\displaystyle\{[-2x_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},\varepsilon_{3}+iy_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{0,1/2\},\quad 0\leq x_{2},y_{2},y_{3}\leq 1\},

(55)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{2}\circ F_{2,4})=

\displaystyle\{[x_{1}+ix_{1},x_{2}-ix_{2},x_{3}+i\varepsilon_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{0,1/2\},\quad 0\leq x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\leq 1\},

(56)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\xi_{2}\circ\eta_{2}\circ F_{2,4})=

\displaystyle\{[x_{1}-ix_{1},x_{2}+ix_{2},x_{3}+i\varepsilon_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{0,1/2\},\quad 0\leq x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\leq 1\}.

(57)

Equality (
4
) implies that
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
and
\operatorname{Fix}(\iota\circ\kappa)
consist of four connected components each, two at
t=0
and two at
t=1/2
. These are diffeomorphic to a torus
T^{3}
.
The singular locus of
X_{2,4}^{1}
is
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
. We denote by
N_{1}
(respectively
N_{2}
) the connected component at
t=0
determined by
\varepsilon_{3}=0
(respectively
\varepsilon_{3}=1/2
), and by
N_{3}
(respectively
N_{4}
) the connected component at
t=1/2
determined by
\varepsilon_{3}=0
(respectively
\varepsilon_{3}=1/2
).

In both cases,
k=1,2
, the involution
\iota
preserves every connected component of
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
. Under all our identifications, the action of
\iota
on the tori reverses the sign of the first two coordinates and preserves the last. Hence, all the connected components of

\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)/\iota
are diffeomorphic to
T^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times S^{1}
, where
T^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is the pillowcase orbifold (it is homeomorphic to
S^{2}
). Similarly, each connected component of

\operatorname{Fix}(\iota\circ\kappa)/\iota
are orbifolds
T^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times S^{1}
. The set
\operatorname{Fix}(\iota\circ\kappa)\cap\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)=\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)\cap\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
is the singular locus of
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)/\iota
. It consists of eight circles if
k=1
, and sixteen circles if
k=2
; in both cases, half of these lie on
t=0
. Taking into account the descriptions of the singular locus of
X_{k,a}
, we deduce that
\operatorname{Fix}(\iota\circ\kappa)\cup\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)/\iota
has two connected components if
k=1
and four if
k=2
. These components are diffeomorphic to
\Sigma^{\vee}\times S^{1}
, where
\Sigma^{\vee}
is consists of
2
pillowcase orbifolds that intersect each other at their four singular points.
If
k=1
, these are determined by the parameter values
t=0,1/2
and if
k=2
, these are described by the pair
(t,\varepsilon_{3})\in\{0,1/2\}^{2}
.

Next Lemma describes
\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)
on
M_{1,a}
and
M_{2,a}
:

Lemma 41
.

1.

The fixed locus of
\iota
on
M_{1,a}
,
a=3,6
consists of
4
connected components
L_{1},L_{2},L_{3},L_{4}
. The component
L_{1}
is the mapping torus of
\mathrm{r}_{3}\colon\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{1}\to\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{1}
,
\mathrm{r}_{3}(z)=e^{\frac{2\pi i}{3}}z
. The remaining are diffeomorphic to a
3
-torus.
The involution
\kappa
preserves all these components.

2.

The fixed locus of
\iota
on
M_{2,4}
consists of
10
connected components
L_{1},\dots,L_{10}
.
The components
L_{1}
,
L_{2}
,
L_{5}
and
L_{8}
are diffeomorphic to the mapping torus of
-\mathrm{Id}\colon\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{2}\to\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{2}
. The remaining are diffeomorphic to a
3
-torus. In addition,
\kappa
fixes
L_{1},L_{2},L_{3},L_{4}
and swaps
L_{j}
with
L_{j+3}
for
j=5,6,7
.

Proof.

We find
\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)
using equation (
3
). Since
\xi_{k}[z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}]=[-z_{1},-z_{2},z_{3}]
, we have:

\operatorname{Fix}(\iota\circ\kappa)=q([0,1]\times\{[p_{1},p_{2},z],\quad 2p_{1},2p_{2}\in\Gamma_{k},\quad z\in\mathbb{C}\}).

To describe its connected components, we define
\mathrm{r}_{a}\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
,
\mathrm{r}_{a}(z)=e^{\frac{2\pi i}{a}}z
, so that

F_{k,a}[z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}]=[\mathrm{r}_{a}(z_{1}),\mathrm{r}_{a}(z_{1}-z_{2}),\mathrm{r}_{a}^{-2}(z_{3})]
, and we set
\mathrm{h}\colon(\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})^{2}\to(\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})^{2}
,
\mathrm{h}[z_{1},z_{2}]=[\mathrm{r}_{a}(z_{1}),\mathrm{r}_{a}(z_{1}-z_{2})]
.

Let
p_{1},p_{2}\in\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{k}
, and let
s\geq 1
such that

\mathrm{h}^{-s}[p_{1},p_{2}]=[p_{1},p_{2}]
and
\mathrm{h}^{-j}[p_{1},p_{2}]\neq[p_{1},p_{2}]
for
0<j<s
. The connected component containing
C_{[p_{1},p_{2}]}=q([0,1]\times\{[p_{1},p_{2},z],\,z\in\mathbb{C}\}
is

\mathcal{C}_{[p_{1},p_{2}]}=q([0,s]\times\{[p_{1},p_{2},z],\quad z\in\mathbb{C}\})=\bigcup_{j=0}^{s-1}C_{\mathrm{h}^{-j}[p_{1},p_{2}]},

which is diffeomorphic to the mapping torus of
r_{a}^{-2s}\colon\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k}\to\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k}
. Before computing the connected components, we observe that if
p_{1},p_{2}\in\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{k}
then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{h}^{2r}[p_{1},p_{2}]=[\mathrm{r}_{a}^{2r}(p_{1}),\mathrm{r}_{a}^{2r}(p_{2})],\qquad\mathrm{h}^{2r+1}[p_{1},p_{2}]=[\mathrm{r}_{a}^{2r+1}(p_{1}),\mathrm{r}_{a}^{2r+1}(p_{1}+p_{2})].

(58)

In addition,
\kappa
maps
\mathcal{C}_{[p_{1},p_{2}]}
into
\mathcal{C}_{[p_{1}^{\prime},p_{2}^{\prime}]}
if and only if

\underline{\kappa}[p_{1},p_{2}]=[\overline{p}_{1},\overline{p}_{2}+\overline{p}_{1}]
transforms
\{h^{s}[p_{1},p_{2}],\,s\geq 0\}
into
\{h^{s}[p_{1},p_{2}],\,s\geq 0\}
. Here we used
[-\overline{p}]=[\overline{p}]
if
2p\in\Gamma_{k}
.

We first deal with the cases
k=1
and
a=3,6
. The classes of
\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{1}/\Gamma_{1}
are determined by
0,\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}e^{\frac{\pi i}{3}},\frac{1}{2}e^{\frac{2\pi i}{3}}
. First,
h[0,0]=[0,0]=\underline{\kappa}[0,0]
so that the component
L_{1}=\mathcal{C}_{[0,0]}=C_{[0,0]}
is fixed by
\kappa
, and is the mapping torus of
r_{a}^{-2}
; these are both diffeomorphic because
r_{3}^{-2}=(r_{6}^{-2})^{-1}
. To deal with the remaining components, we observe:

\displaystyle[1/2]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{r}_{3}}[e^{\frac{2\pi i}{3}}/2]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{r}_{3}}[e^{\frac{\pi i}{3}}/2]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{r}_{3}}[1/2],\qquad[1/2]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{r}_{6}}[e^{\frac{\pi i}{3}}/2]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{r}_{6}}[e^{\frac{2\pi i}{3}}/2]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{r}_{6}}[1/2].

(59)

Therefore,
\mathrm{r}_{a}^{3}=\mathrm{Id}
on
\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{1}/\Gamma
. This implies
\mathrm{h}^{3}[p_{1},p_{2}]=[p_{1},p_{1}+p_{2}]
and

\mathrm{h}^{6}=\mathrm{Id}
on
(\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{1}/\Gamma)^{2}
.
With this information, we distinguish two cases:

1.

If
[p_{1}]=[0]
and
[p_{2}]\neq[0]
, then
\mathrm{h}^{3}[0,p_{2}]=[0,p_{2}]
, and
\mathrm{h}^{k}[0,p_{2}]\neq[0,p_{2}]
for
k=1,2
. The connected component

L_{2}=\mathcal{C}_{[0,1/2]}

is diffeomorphic to a torus
S^{1}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{1}
, and it is preserved by
\kappa
as
\underline{\kappa}[0,p_{2}]\in\{[0,p_{2}],\,2p_{2}\in\Gamma_{1}\}
.

2.

Let
[p_{1}]\neq[0]
, we claim that
\mathrm{h}^{j}[p_{1},p_{2}]\neq[p_{1},p_{2}]
for
1\leq j\leq 5
. For
j\neq 3
, this holds because the first component of
\mathrm{h}^{j}[p_{1},0]
is
\mathrm{r}_{a}^{j}[p_{1}]\neq[p_{1}]
by equation (
59
), and
\mathrm{h}^{3}[p_{1},p_{2}]=[p_{1},p_{1}+p_{2}]\neq[p_{1},p_{2}]
. Indeed, equations (
58
) and (
59
) ensure:

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{h}^{s}[1/2,0],\,0\leq s\leq 5\}=

\displaystyle\{[p_{1},0],\,[p_{1}]\neq 0\}\cup\{[p_{1},p_{1}],\,[p_{1}]\neq 0\}\},

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{h}^{s}[1/2,e^{\frac{\pi i}{3}}/2],\,0\leq s\leq 5\}=

\displaystyle\{[p_{1},p_{2}],\,[p_{1}]\neq[p_{2}],[p_{1}]\neq 0,[p_{2}]\neq[0]\}.

This yields two connected components diffeomorphic to
S^{1}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{1}
, namely
L_{3}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2,0]}
, and
L_{4}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2,e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}}/2]}
. One can easily check that
\kappa
preserves these components.

We finally deal with
k=2
and
a=4
. Note that
\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{2}/\Gamma_{2}=\{[0],[\frac{1}{2}],[\frac{i}{2}],[\frac{1+i}{2}]\}
. In addition,

\displaystyle[1/2]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{r}_{4}}[i/2]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{r}_{4}}[1/2],\qquad[1/2+i/2]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{r}_{4}}[1/2+i/2].

(60)

Therefore,
\mathrm{r}_{4}^{2}=\mathrm{Id}
on
\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{2}/\Gamma
, so that
\mathrm{h}^{2}=\mathrm{Id}
on
(\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{2}/\Gamma)^{2}
by equation (
58
). We also observe:

\displaystyle[\mathrm{r}_{4}(p_{1})+1/2+i/2]=[p_{1}]\qquad\mbox{ if }p_{1}=1/2,i/2.

(61)

Equations (
60
) and (
61
) allows us to deduce
\mathrm{h}[p_{1},p_{2}]=[p_{1},p_{2}]
if

[p_{1},p_{2}]\in\{[0,0],[0,1/2+i/2],[1/2+i/2,1/2],[1/2+i/2,i/2]\}
. This yields four components,
\mathcal{C}_{[0,0]}
,
\mathcal{C}_{[0,1/2+i/2]}
,
\mathcal{C}_{[1/2+i/2,1/2]}
,
\mathcal{C}_{[1/2+i/2,i/2]}
diffeomorphic to the mapping torus of
\mathrm{r}_{4}^{2}=-\mathrm{Id}
. We now check that the remaining
12
points are not fixed by
\mathrm{h}
; so that we obtain
6
connected components diffeomorphic to
S^{1}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{2}
.
We distinguish the cases:

1.

If
[p_{1}]=[1/2],[i/2]
, equation (
60
) ensures that
\mathrm{h}[p_{1},p_{2}]\neq[p_{1},p_{2}]
.
These points determine
4
connected components

\mathcal{C}_{[1/2,p_{2}]}=\mathcal{C}_{[i/2,i/2+p_{2}]}
, with
[p_{2}]\in\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{2}
.

2.

If
[p_{1}]=[0]
and
[p_{2}]=[1/2],[i/2]
, we have
\mathrm{h}[0,p_{2}]=[0,\mathrm{r}_{4}(p_{2})]\neq[0,p_{2}]
, yielding
\mathcal{C}_{[0,1/2]}=\mathcal{C}_{[0,i/2]}
.

3.

If

[p_{1}]=[1/2+i/2]
and
[p_{2}]=[0],[1/2+i/2]
, then
\mathrm{h}[p_{1},p_{2}]=[p_{1},p_{2}+p_{1}]\neq[p_{1},p_{2}]
, providing us with

\mathcal{C}_{[1/2+i/2,0]}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2+i/2,1/2+i/2]}
.

In this lattice,
\underline{\kappa}[p_{1},p_{2}]=[p_{1},p_{1}+p_{2}]
. From this expression one can easily check that the components

L_{1}=\mathcal{C}_{[0,0]},L_{2}=\mathcal{C}_{[0,1/2+1/2i]},L_{3}=\mathcal{C}_{[0,1/2]},L_{4}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2+i/2,0]}
remain fixed under
\kappa
.
In addition,
\kappa
swaps
L_{5}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2+i/2,1/2]}
with
L_{8}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2+i/2,i/2]}
,
and
L_{6}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2,0]}
with
L_{9}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2,1/2]}
, and
L_{7}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2,i/2]}
with
L_{10}=\mathcal{C}_{[1/2,1/2+i/2]}
.
∎

Remark 18
.

In all the cases,
L_{1}
is preserved under
\iota
. Indeed,
H^{1}(L_{1},\mathbb{R})=\langle[dt]\rangle\oplus K^{1}
according to
[
4
, Lemma 12]
. In both cases,
K^{1}=\{0\}
; so that
H^{1}(L_{1})^{\kappa}=\{0\}
. Therefore,
L_{1}
does not have a nowhere-vanishing closed
1
-form. When resolving
X_{k,a}^{2}
, we will therefore desingularize

M_{k,a}/\iota
first.

We now describe the singular locus of
Z_{2,4}
.
It is clear that
\operatorname{Fix}(\gamma)=\emptyset
if
\gamma\in\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle
. We also have
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa\circ\mathrm{j}_{1}\circ\char 17\relax_{2})=\emptyset
, as

\zeta_{1}\circ\zeta_{2}\circ\eta_{2}=[-\bar{z}_{1},\bar{z}_{1}+\bar{z}_{2},-\bar{z}_{3}+1/2+i/{2}]
and
\zeta_{1}\circ\zeta_{2}\circ\eta_{2}\circ F_{2,4}=[i\bar{z}_{1},-i\bar{z}_{2},\bar{z}_{3}+1/2+i/2]
. These maps do not fix any point, as they involve the translations
y_{3}\mapsto y_{3}+1/2
and
x_{3}\mapsto x_{3}+1/2
, respectively.
The singular locus is then determined by
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
,
\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{1}\circ\kappa)
and
\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{2}\circ\kappa)
. The first set is deduced from equations (
54
) and (
56
); the remaining are determined by:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\zeta_{1}\circ\eta_{2})=

\displaystyle\{[-2x_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},\varepsilon_{3}+iy_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{-1/4,1/4\},\quad 0\leq x_{2},y_{2},y_{3}\leq 1\},

(62)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\zeta_{2}\circ\eta_{2}\circ F_{2,4})=

\displaystyle\{[x_{1}-ix_{1},x_{2}+ix_{2},x_{3}+i\varepsilon_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{-1/4,1/4\},\quad 0\leq x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\leq 1\},

(63)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\zeta_{1}\circ\eta_{2}\circ F_{2,4})=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\zeta_{2}\circ\eta_{2})=\emptyset.

(64)

Again,
\zeta_{1}\circ\eta_{2}\circ F_{2,4}
and
\zeta_{2}\circ\eta_{2}
involve translations in the variables
x_{3}
and
y_{3}
, respectively. We denote the components of the fixed locus at
t=0
by
N_{1}^{1},N_{1}^{2},N_{2}^{1},N_{2}^{2}
, where
N_{1}^{k}\subset\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
and the upper index
k=1
corresponds to
\varepsilon_{3}=0
or
\varepsilon_{3}=-1/4
. The components at
t=1/2
are denoted by
N_{3}^{1},N_{3}^{2},N_{4}^{1},N_{4}^{2}
, where
N_{3}^{k}\subset\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
and we use the same convention for the upper index
k
. All these are diffeomorphic to a torus
T^{3}
. The involution

\mathrm{j}_{1}
swaps
N_{3}^{1}
with
N_{3}^{2}
and
N_{4}^{1}
with
N_{4}^{2}
, and it
preserves every connected component at
t=0
. Similarly,
\mathrm{j}_{2}
interchanges
N_{j}^{1}
with
N_{j}^{2}
for
j=1,2
and preserves
N_{j}^{k}
where
j=3,4
. Hence, the singular locus of
Z_{2,4}
consists of
4
components that we denote by
N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}
. We have a
2:1
cover
N_{j}^{1}\to N_{j}
; indeed
H^{1}(N_{1})=H^{1}(N_{2})=\langle[dy_{3}]\rangle
and
H^{1}(N_{3})=H^{1}(N_{4})=\langle[dx_{3}]\rangle
.

We observe that
the projection
M_{2,4}\to M_{2,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle
induces a branched covering.
X_{2,4}^{1}\to Z_{2,4}
. The ramification locus is the projection of
\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{1}\circ\kappa)\cup\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{2}\circ\kappa)
to
X_{2,4}
. It is also worth pointing out that the map
F_{2,4}
descends to
T^{6}/\langle\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\rangle
. Indeed,

M_{2,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle
is the mapping torus of
F_{2,4}\colon T^{6}/\langle\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\rangle\to T^{6}/\langle\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\rangle
.

We finally compute the singular locus of
X_{3,4}^{1}
and
Z_{3,4}
. The singular locus of
X_{3,4}^{1}
is the projection of
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)\subset M_{3,4}
and it is determined by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{3})=

\displaystyle\sqcup_{n=0}^{1}\{[n-2iy_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},\varepsilon_{3}+iy_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{0,1/2\},\quad 0\leq x_{2},y_{2},y_{3}\leq 1\},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{3}\circ F_{3,4})=

\displaystyle\sqcup_{n=0}^{1}\{[x_{1}+i(x_{1}+n),x_{2}-ix_{2},x_{3}+i\varepsilon_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{0,1/2\},\quad 0\leq x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\leq 1\}.

Therefore, there are
8
connected components, diffeomorphic to a
3
-torus. We denote by
N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}
the components at
t=0
and
N_{5},N_{6},N_{7},N_{8}
those at
t=1/2
.
Whereas for
Z_{3,4}
, similar computations show that it is determined by the projection
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)\cup\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{1}\circ\kappa)\cup\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{2}\circ\kappa)\subset M_{3,4}
. The sets
\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{1}\circ\kappa)
,
\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{2}\circ\kappa)
are induced by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\zeta_{1}\circ\eta_{3})=

\displaystyle\sqcup_{n=0}^{1}\{[-2x_{2}+in,x_{2}+iy_{2},\varepsilon_{3}+iy_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{-1/4,1/4\},\quad 0\leq x_{2},y_{2},y_{3}\leq 1\},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\zeta_{2}\circ\eta_{3}\circ F_{2,4})=

\displaystyle\sqcup_{n=0}^{1}\{[x_{1}-i(x_{1}+n),x_{2}+ix_{2},x_{3}+i\varepsilon_{3}],\quad\varepsilon_{3}\in\{-1/4,1/4\},\quad 0\leq x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\leq 1\},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\zeta_{1}\circ\eta_{3}\circ F_{2,4})=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fix}(\zeta_{2}\circ\eta_{2})=\emptyset.

A similar argument shows that the singular locus of
Z_{3,4}
consists of eight connected components, each of which is double-covered by
T^{3}
. We denote by
N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}
the components at
t=0
and by
N_{5},N_{6},N_{7},N_{8}
those at
t=1
.

Lemma 42
.

There are double covers
X_{3,4}^{1}\to X_{2,4}^{1}
and
Z_{3,4}\to Z_{2,4}
.

Proof.

Consider the map

\mathrm{T}\colon M_{3,4}\to M_{3,4}
,
\mathrm{T}[t,[z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}]]=[t,[z_{1}+1,z_{2},z_{3}]],

and note that
M_{3,4}/\mathrm{T}=M_{2,4}
and the action of
\mathrm{T}
is free on
M_{3,4}
.
There is an induced
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
action
\mathrm{T}\colon X_{3,4}^{1}\to X_{3,4}^{1}
because

(\mathrm{T}\circ\kappa)[t,[z]]=[1-t,[-\bar{z}_{1}+1,\bar{z}_{2}+\bar{z}_{1},-\bar{z}_{3}]]=[1-t,[-\bar{z}_{1}-1,\bar{z}_{2}+\bar{z}_{1}+1,-\bar{z}_{3}]]=(\kappa\circ\mathrm{T})[t,[z]].

(65)

One checks that
\mathrm{T}\circ\mathrm{j}_{p}=\mathrm{j}_{p}\circ\mathrm{T}
in a similar way. Therefore,
\mathrm{T}\colon Z_{3,4}\to Z_{3,4}
is a well-defined
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action, and we have
X_{2,4}^{1}=X_{3,4}^{1}/\mathrm{T}
,
Z_{2,4}=Z_{3,4}/\mathrm{T}
. In addition, from equation (
65
) we deduce that the action of
\mathrm{T}
is free outside the singular locus. From the computations of
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
and
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa\circ\mathrm{j}_{p})
on
M_{3,4}
, we deduce that
\mathrm{T}
is also free on the singular locus.
∎

5.2.2
Resolutions

Theorem 3.11 in
[
29
]
yields resolutions
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{k,a}\to X^{1}_{k,a}

k=1
,
a=3,6
because the singular locus admits a nowhere-vanishing closed
1
-form. In addition, Lemma
37
allows us to find resolutions

\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4}\to X_{3,4}^{1}
,
\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}\to Z_{3,4}
, and lifts
\widetilde{T}\colon\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1}\to\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1}
,
\widetilde{T}\colon\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}\to\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}
of the map induced by
\mathrm{T}\colon M_{3,4}\to M_{3,4}
,
\mathrm{T}[t,[z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}]]=[t,[z_{1}+1,z_{2},z_{3}]]
defined in the proof of Lemma (
42
). This result implies that
\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{1}=\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1}/\widetilde{T}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}=\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}/\widetilde{T}
are the closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
resolutions of
X_{2,4}^{1}
and
Z_{2,4}
. Of course,
\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1}\to\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{1}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}\to\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}
are double covers.

To deal with
X^{2}_{k,a}
, we first desingularize the closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
orbifold
(Y_{k,a}=M_{k,a}/\iota,\varphi)
.
The nowhere-vanishing
1
-form we use is
\theta=dt|_{\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)}
on
M_{1,a}
, and
\theta|_{L_{j}}=dt|_{L_{j}}
if
1\leq j\leq 7
and
\theta|_{L_{j}}=-dt|_{L_{j}}

j=8,9,10
on
M_{2,4}
. It satisfies
\kappa^{*}\theta=-\theta
on
\mathcal{L}_{F}
and
\kappa^{*}\theta=\theta
on
\mathcal{L}_{s}
. Theorem
37
allows us to obtain
a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
resolution
\rho_{1}\colon\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}\to Y_{k,a}
and a lift
\tilde{\kappa}\colon\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}\to\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}
.

Remark 19
.

Let
S_{k}
be the algebraic resolution of
T^{4}_{k}=(\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, which is a
K3
surface. The biholomorphism
T_{k}^{4}
given by
[z_{1},z_{2}]\to[\mathrm{r}_{a}(z_{1}),\mathrm{r}_{a}(z_{2}-z_{1})]
lifts to a biholomorphism
\widetilde{f}_{k,a}\colon S_{k}\to S_{k}
. It turns out that
\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}
is diffeomorphic to the mapping torus of
\widetilde{F}_{k,a}=(\widetilde{f}_{k,a},\mathrm{r}_{a}^{-2})\colon S_{k}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k}\to S_{k}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k}
.
This occurs because the resolution procedure consists of blowing up the coordinates
[z_{1},z_{2}]
around the points
[p_{1},p_{2}]
with
2p_{1},2p_{2}\in\Gamma_{k}
, as
\iota[t,z]=[t,-z_{1},-z_{2},z_{3}]
.

Lemma 43
.

All connected components of the fixed locus of
\tilde{\kappa}
in
\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}
are diffeomorphic to
\Sigma\times S^{1}
, where
\Sigma
is a surface of genus
3
.
The number of connected components of
\operatorname{Fix}(\tilde{\kappa})
is
2
if
k=1
, and
4
if
k=2
.

Proof.

Since
\rho\circ\kappa=\tilde{\kappa}\circ\rho
, it follows that
\operatorname{Fix}(\tilde{\kappa})
is the strict transform of
(\operatorname{Fix}(\iota\circ\kappa)\cup\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa))/\iota
. The connected components of this set are diffeomorphic to
