\Sigma^{\vee}\times S^{1}
, where
\Sigma^{\vee}
is a union of two pillowcase orbifolds that intersect each other at their singular points. In addition,
\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)\cap\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)
is the union of the products of circles with the singular points of
\Sigma^{\vee}
.
Given one such component,
\{[\varepsilon,p_{1},p_{2}]\}\times S^{1}\subset\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)\cap\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
, where
\varepsilon\in\{0,1/2\}
, and
2p_{k}\in\Gamma_{k}
,
the coordinates
[z_{1},z_{2}]
of
\operatorname{Fix}(\iota\circ\kappa)\cup\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
form two transversal real
2
-planes in
\mathbb{C}^{2}
. Indeed, from the formulas in section
5.2.1
, we can check that one of these real two planes can be obtained from the other by multiplying by
i
.
Since the strict transform of those planes after a blow-up in
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is
\mathbb{RP}^{1}
, in the resolution we replace
\{[\varepsilon,p_{1},p_{2}]\}\times S^{1}
with
\mathbb{RP}^{1}\times S^{1}
. Therefore, the strict transform of
S^{1}\times\Sigma^{\vee}

is
S^{1}\times\Sigma
, where
\Sigma
a union of two
spheres, each of these punctured at four discs, and connected through cylinders. Hence,
\Sigma
is a surface of genus
3
.
∎

We denote the connected components of
\operatorname{Fix}(\widetilde{\kappa})
on
\widetilde{Y}_{1,a}
at
t=0
and
t=1/2
by
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
respectively. For
\widetilde{Y}_{2,4}
, we label the connected components at
t=0
by

K_{1},K_{2}
, and those at
t=1/2
by
K_{3},K_{4}
.

Lemma
43
, and Theorem
37
guarantee that the hypotheses of
[
29
, Theorem 3.11]
hold for
\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}/\tilde{\kappa}
. Hence, there is a closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
resolution
\rho_{2}\colon\widetilde{X}_{k,a}^{2}\to\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}/\widetilde{\kappa}
. Indeed, let
\bar{\rho}_{1}\colon\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}/\tilde{\kappa}\to X^{2}_{k,a}=Y_{k,a}/\kappa
be the map induced by
\rho_{1}
. Then the resolution map is
\bar{\rho}_{1}\circ\rho_{2}
and we
have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}/\tilde{\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{X}_{k,a}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k,a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}_{k,a},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\rho}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{2}}

(66)

where the horizontal arrows on the left are the quotient maps.

5.3
Topological properties of the constructed manifolds

The proof of Theorem
1
is contained in Propositions
44
,
45
,
52
, Corollary
51
, the discussion at the end of section
5.3.3
, and Theorem
54
.
Excluding the fundamental group, the properties listed in table
1
are shown in Theorems
47
,
48
, Propositions
55
,
57
,
59
and
62
.
Along the way, we provide explicit formulas for the cohomology groups and the first Pontryagin class of the constructed examples.

5.3.1
Fundamental group

Making use of Seifert-Van Kampen theorem we obtain
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{X}^{1}_{k,a})\cong\pi_{1}(X^{1}_{k,a})
,
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{Z}_{k,4})\cong\pi_{1}({Z}_{k,4})
and
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{X}^{2}_{k,a})\cong\pi_{1}(\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}/\tilde{\kappa})
. To compute the fundamental group of an orbifold
M/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
we use
[
7
, Chapter 2, Corollary 6.3]
, which implies that if an involution
\kappa\colon M\to M
has a fixed point, then
the map

\mathrm{pr}_{*}\colon\pi_{1}(M)\to\pi_{1}(M/\kappa)
is surjective. For this calculation, we will frequently use that if a loop
\gamma\colon[0,1]\to M
satisfies
\kappa\circ\gamma(s)=\gamma^{-1}(s)
for every
s\in[0,1]
, then there is a base-point preserving homotopy from
q\circ\gamma
to the constant loop. The reason is that
\mathrm{pr}\circ\gamma(s)=\mathrm{pr}\circ\bar{\gamma}(s)
, where

\bar{\gamma}\colon[0,1]\to M,\,\bar{\gamma}(s)=\begin{cases}\gamma(s),&s\leq 1/2,\\
\gamma(1-s),&s\geq 1/2.\end{cases}

and
\bar{\gamma}
is homotopic to the a constant loop. The homotopy
H_{r}(s)
between these consists of two parts: we follow
\gamma
from
\gamma(0)
to
\gamma(r/2)
and then reverse that path. Finally, we compute the fundamental group of
M_{k,a}
, and
\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}
using equation (
5
) in section
2.1
and Remark
19
. To describe these, we introduce some notations.
Let
\mathrm{i}_{p}\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}^{3}
be the inclusion to the
p
-th component,
p=1,2,3
. A set of generators of
\pi_{1}(T^{6}_{k},[0])
is determined by the homotopy classes of the loops
\gamma_{p,k}
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{p,1},\gamma_{p,2}\colon[0,1]\to T^{6}_{1},\quad\gamma_{p,1}=[\mathrm{i}_{p}(t)],\quad\gamma_{p,2}=[\mathrm{i}_{p}(te^{\frac{2\pi i}{3}})],

\displaystyle p=1,2,3,

(67)

\displaystyle\gamma_{p,1},\gamma_{p,2}\colon[0,1]\to T^{6}_{2},\quad\gamma_{p,1}=[\mathrm{i}_{p}(t)],\quad\gamma_{p,2}=[\mathrm{i}_{p}(ti)],

\displaystyle p=1,2,3.

(68)

Taking into account Remark
19
, that the
K3
surface
S_{k}
is simply connected, and abusing slightly of the notation, we say that
[\gamma_{3,1}],[\gamma_{3,2}]
generate
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})=\pi_{1}(S_{k}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})
.
Given a loop
\rho\colon[0.1]\to T^{6}_{k}
or
\rho\colon[0.1]\to S_{k}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k}
we denote by
\tilde{\rho}

its inclusion to the mapping torus at
t=\frac{1}{2}
; that is,
\tilde{\rho}(t)=[\frac{1}{2},\rho]
. We consider
\tilde{\gamma}_{0}
the loop on
M_{k,a}
or
Y_{k,a}
given by equation (
6
). In particular,
\pi_{1}(M_{k,a})
is generated by
\tilde{\gamma}_{0}
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{p,q}
and the generators are subject to the relations:

[\tilde{\rho}]=[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}](F_{*}[\tilde{\rho}])[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},\qquad[\rho]\in\pi_{1}(T^{6}_{k}).

(69)

By an abuse of notation, we denote the projection maps by
\mathrm{pr}\colon M_{k,a}\to X^{j}_{k,a}
,
\mathrm{pr}\colon\widetilde{Y}^{j}_{k,a}\to\widetilde{Y}^{j}_{k,a}/\tilde{\kappa}
. We first deal with the orbifolds obtained from
M_{1,3}
and
M_{1,6}
:

Proposition 44
.

The manifolds
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{1,6}
,
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{1,3}
,
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{1,6}
are simply connected, and

\pi_{1}(\widetilde{X}^{1}_{1,3})=\mathbb{Z}_{3}
.

Proof.

We first compute
\pi_{1}(M_{1,3})
. For that, we analyze the map
(F_{1,3})_{*}\colon\pi_{1}(T^{6}_{1})\to\pi_{1}(T^{6}_{1})
in terms of the generators
\gamma_{p,1}
described in equation (
67
):

\displaystyle(F_{1,3})_{*}[\gamma_{p,1}]=

\displaystyle[\gamma_{p,2}],\qquad\qquad(F_{1,3})_{*}[\gamma_{p,2}]=-[\gamma_{p,1}]-[\gamma_{p,2}],\quad p\neq 1,

\displaystyle(F_{1,3})_{*}[\gamma_{1,1}]=

\displaystyle[\gamma_{1,2}]-[\gamma_{2,2}],\quad(F_{1,3})_{*}[\gamma_{1,2}]=-[\gamma_{1,1}]-[\gamma_{1,2}]+[\gamma_{2,1}]+[\gamma_{2,2}].

From the relations (
69
) we obtain:

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{p,1}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{p,2}][\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{p,2}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{p,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{p,2}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},\qquad p\neq 1,

(70)

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}][\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}][\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}][\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1}.

(71)

We now claim that

H_{1}(M_{1,3})=\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3}
.
To verify this, consider
c_{0},c_{p,q}\in H^{1}(M_{1,3})
the homology classes determined by
[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]
and
[\tilde{\gamma}_{p,q}]
respectively. If
p\neq 1
, equality (
70
) implies
c_{p,2}=c_{p,1}
and
2c_{p,2}=-c_{p,1}
; therefore,
c_{p,1}
has order
3
. If
p=1
, equality (
71
) shows
c_{1,1}=c_{1,2}-c_{2,2}
and
2c_{1,2}=-c_{1,1}+2c_{2,2}
; therefore
c_{1,1}
and
c_{1,2}
have order
3
. This implies the claimed equality, and the genertors are
c_{0}
and
c_{p,1}
,
p=1,2,3
.

Observe that
\kappa\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(s)=\tilde{\gamma}_{0}^{-1}(s)
, and
\kappa\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{p,1}(s)=[\frac{1}{2},\eta\circ\gamma_{p,1}(s)]
. From the second expression we deduce
\kappa\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}(s)=\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}^{-1}(s)
. Using the observation at the beginning of the section we obtain
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]=1
and
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]=1
. Equality (
70
) yields:

\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}],\qquad\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}]^{2}=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]^{-1},\qquad\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]=1.

In particular, the order of
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]
is either zero or
3
.
In addition, on
M_{1,3}
we also have
\kappa_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]=[\gamma_{1,1}]^{-1}[\gamma_{2,1}]
; hence

\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]^{2}=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]
and the order of
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]
coincides with that of
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]
. Equation (
71
) implies
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}]=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}]=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]^{3}=1
. Hence, there is a surjection

\mathbb{Z}_{3}\to\pi_{1}({X}^{1}_{1,3})
that maps
1
to
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]
. In particular,
\pi_{1}({X}^{1}_{1,3})
is either
\{1\}
or
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and
\pi_{1}(X_{1,3})=H_{1}(X_{1,3},\mathbb{Z})
.

To prove that
\pi_{1}({X}^{1}_{1,3})=\mathbb{Z}_{3}
it suffices to check that
H_{1}(X_{1,3}^{1},\mathbb{Z}_{3})\neq\{0\}
because the
universal coefficient theorem for homology ensures that
H_{1}(X_{1,3}^{1},\mathbb{Z}_{3})=H_{1}(X_{1,3}^{1},\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{3}
, and the right hand side is either
\{0\}
or
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
according to our previous argument.
In addition,
H_{1}(X_{1,3}^{1},\mathbb{Z}_{3})=H_{1}(M_{1,3},\mathbb{Z}_{3})^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
by
[
7
, Chapter 3, Theorem 2.4]
. A new application of the universal coefficient theorem yields

H_{1}(M_{1,3},\mathbb{Z}_{3})=H_{1}(M_{1,3},\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{3}=(\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{3}=\mathbb{Z}_{3}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3},

this group is spanned by
c_{0}
and
c_{p,1}
for
p=1,2,3
. The action of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is determined by:

c_{0}\mapsto-c_{0},\quad c_{1,1}\mapsto-c_{1,1}+c_{2,1},\quad c_{2,1}\mapsto c_{2,1},\quad c_{3,1}\mapsto-c_{3,1}.

Hence,
c_{2,1}\in H_{1}(M_{1,3},\mathbb{Z}_{3})^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
and therefore,

\pi_{1}(X_{1,3})=\mathbb{Z}_{3}
. We now show that
X_{1,6}
is simply connected. First, the generators
[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]
,
[\tilde{\gamma}_{p,q}]
, of
\pi_{1}(M^{1}_{1,6})
satisfy the relations:

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}][\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}][\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},\qquad

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}][\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

(72)

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}][\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}][\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},\qquad

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

(73)

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},\qquad

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}][\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1}.

(74)

We again have
\kappa\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{0}(s)=\tilde{\gamma}_{0}^{-1}(s)
, and
\kappa\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{p,1}(s)=[\frac{1}{2},\eta\circ\gamma_{p,1}(s)]
. In particular,
\kappa\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}(s)=\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}^{-1}(s)
. Therefore
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]=1
and
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]=1
. Equality (
74
) yields
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]=1
and (
73
) implies
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}]=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]=1
. Taking these equalities into account together with equation (
72
), we obtain
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}]=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]=1
.
Hence
\pi_{1}(X_{1,6})=\{1\}
.

If
a=3,6
, then
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{Y}_{1,k})
is generated by
[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]
,

[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]
and
[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]
. They satisfy the relations:

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &=[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}][\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},&[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]&=[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},&k=3,\\
[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]&=[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},&[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]&=[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}][\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},&k=6.\end{aligned}\end{cases}

In both cases,
\tilde{\kappa}\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{0}=\tilde{\gamma}_{0}^{-1}
and
\tilde{\kappa}\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}=\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}^{-1}
. Hence,
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]=1
, which implies
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]=1
. Thus,
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{Y}_{k,3}/\tilde{\kappa})=\{1\}
.
∎

We now focus on the orbifolds obtained from
{M}_{2,4}
and
{M}_{3,4}
.

Proposition 45
.

The manifolds
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4}
,
\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}
,
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{2,4}
,
\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}

are simply connected and
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{X}^{1}_{2,4})=\pi_{1}(\widetilde{Z}_{2,4})=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. In particular,
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}
are, respectively, the universal cover of
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{2,4}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}
.

Proof.

For
\pi_{1}({M}_{2,4})
, we deduce the following relations for the generators
[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]
,
[\tilde{\gamma}_{p,q}]
:

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}][\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}][\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

(75)

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}][\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{2,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

(76)

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]=

\displaystyle[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1}.

(77)

Similar to the proof of Proposition
44
, we obtain the following relations on
X_{2,4}^{1}
:

\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]=1,\quad\quad\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{2,2}]=1,\quad\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]=1.

(78)

Combining them with equation (
75
), (
76
) and (
77
), we find that the projections of the generators to
\pi_{1}(X_{2,4}^{1})
are trivial except possibly for
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,1}]=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,2}]
and
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]
, whose order is at most
2
. We now show that
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]=1
. On
M_{2,4}
, the loop
\gamma_{3,2}
is homotopic to
\gamma_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\gamma_{3,2}^{\prime}\cdot(\gamma_{0}^{\prime})^{-1}
, where

\gamma_{0}^{\prime},\gamma_{3,2}^{\prime}\colon[0,1]\to M_{2,4},\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime}(t)=[(1+t)/2,[0,0,0]],\quad\gamma_{3,2}^{\prime}(t)=[1,[0,0,it]].

Since
\kappa\circ\gamma_{3,2}^{\prime}(t)=[0,[0,0,it]]=[1,[0,0,-it]]=(\gamma_{2,3}^{\prime})^{-1}(t)
,
there is a base-point preserving homotopy from the projection of
[\gamma_{3,2}^{\prime}]
to the constant loop. Hence,
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\gamma_{3,2}]=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\gamma_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\gamma_{3,2}^{\prime}\cdot(\gamma_{0}^{\prime})^{-1}]=1
. Then
\pi_{1}(X_{2,4}^{1})
is either
\{1\}
or
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Lemma
42
guarantees that
\pi_{1}(X_{3,4}^{1})=\{1\}
and
\pi_{1}(X_{2,4}^{1})=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

The generators of
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{Y}_{2,4})
are
[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]
,

[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}],[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]
, and they satisfy

[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,p}]=[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}][\tilde{\gamma}_{3,p}]^{-1}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]^{-1},

for

p=1,2
. From
\tilde{\kappa}\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{0}=\tilde{\gamma}_{0}^{-1}
and
\tilde{\kappa}\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}=\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}^{-1}
, it follows that
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{0}]=\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,1}]=1
. One obtains that
\mathrm{pr}_{*}[\tilde{\gamma}_{3,2}]=1
arguing as in the case of
X_{2,4}^{1}
. Hence,
\pi_{1}(Y_{2,4}/\tilde{\kappa})=\{1\}
.

To compute
\pi_{1}(Z_{2,4})
we recall that there is a branched cover
\mathrm{q}\colon X_{2,4}^{1}\to Z_{2,4}
. In fact,

Z_{2,4}=(X_{2,4}^{1}/{\mathrm{j}}_{1}^{\prime})/{\mathrm{j}}_{2}^{\prime}
, where
{\mathrm{j}}_{k}^{\prime}
are induced by
\mathrm{j}_{k}
. The induced map
\mathrm{q}_{*}\colon\pi_{1}(X_{2,4}^{1})\to\pi_{1}(Z_{2,4})
is surjective, because both maps
\pi_{1}(X_{2,4}^{1})\to\pi_{1}(X_{2,4}^{1}/{\mathrm{j}}_{1}^{\prime})
and
\pi_{1}(X_{2,4}^{1}/{\mathrm{j}}_{1}^{\prime})\to\pi_{1}(Z_{2,4})
are surjective. The reason is that
\operatorname{Fix}({\mathrm{j}}_{1}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
and
\operatorname{Fix}({\mathrm{j}}_{2}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
, as these are the projections of
\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{1}\circ\kappa)
and
\operatorname{Fix}(\mathrm{j}_{2}\circ\kappa)
, respectively. Then, one uses
[
7
, Chapter 2, Corollary 6.3]
. Since
\pi_{1}(X_{2,4}^{1})=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and there is a double cover
Z_{3,4}\to Z_{2,4}
, we conclude that
\pi_{1}(Z_{2,4})=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and
\pi_{1}(Z_{3,4})=\{1\}
.
∎

Remark 20
.

Equalities (
70
) – (
77
) yield:

H_{1}(M_{1,3})=\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3},\quad H_{1}(M_{1,6})=\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{3},\quad H_{1}(M_{2,4})=\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

5.3.2
Cohomology algebra

We find
H^{m}(M_{k,a},\mathbb{R})
for
m=1,2,3
in Proposition
46
, using the description in equation (
7
). We use the notation introduced there.

Proposition 46
.

For
m=1,2,3
,
H^{m}(M_{k,a})=K^{m}_{k,a}\oplus\delta^{*}C^{m-1}_{k,a}
, where

\displaystyle C_{k,a}^{0}=

\displaystyle C^{1}_{k,a}=K^{1}_{k,a}=0,

\displaystyle K^{2}_{k,a}=

\displaystyle\langle i[dz_{1\bar{1}}],i[dz_{3\bar{3}}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{2}}]\rangle,\qquad a\neq 3

\displaystyle K^{2}_{1,3}=

\displaystyle\langle i[dz_{1\bar{1}}],i[dz_{3\bar{3}}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{2}}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{3}}],\mathrm{Im}[dz_{1\bar{3}}]\rangle,

\displaystyle C^{2}_{k,a}=

\displaystyle\langle i[dz_{2\bar{2}}],i[dz_{3\bar{3}}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{2}}]\rangle,\quad a\neq 3,

\displaystyle C^{2}_{1,3}=

\displaystyle\langle i[dz_{2\bar{2}}],i[dz_{3\bar{3}}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{2}}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{2\bar{3}}],\mathrm{Im}[dz_{2\bar{3}}]\rangle,

\displaystyle K^{3}_{1,a}=C^{3}_{1,a}=

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{Re}[dz_{123}],\mathrm{Im}[dz_{123}]\rangle,\quad a=3,6,

\displaystyle K^{3}_{k,4}=C^{3}_{k,4}=

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{Re}[dz_{123}],\mathrm{Im}[dz_{123}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{12\bar{3}}],\mathrm{Im}[dz_{12\bar{3}}]\rangle,\quad k=2,3.

Proof.

Taking into account that
b_{1}(M_{k,a})=1
and
C^{0}=\mathbb{R}
, the short exact sequence (
7
) shows
K^{1}=\{0\}
; therefore,
C^{1}=\{0\}
. Since
F_{k,a}
is a holomorphic map, to obtain
K^{m}
or
C^{m}
, it suffices to compute the kernel or the cokernel of the maps
F_{k,a}^{*}\colon H^{p,q}(T^{6}_{k},\mathbb{C})\to H^{p,q}(T^{6}_{k},\mathbb{C})
with
m=p+q
and
p\geq q
, and then take real parts to conclude.
From now on, we abbreviate
u=e^{\frac{2\pi i}{a}}
and we understand
F=F_{k,a}
; note that
F[z]=[uz_{1},uz_{2}-uz_{1},u^{-2}z_{3}]
.

The
F^{*}
-invariant subspaces of
H^{2,0}(T^{6}_{k})
are
V^{20}_{1}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{12}]\rangle
and
V^{20}_{2}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{13}],[dz_{23}]\rangle
. The map
F^{*}
acts as
u^{2}
on the first, and on the second by

A^{20}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}u^{-1}&-u^{-1}\\
0&u^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

The
F^{*}
-invariant subspaces of

H^{1,1}(T^{6}_{j})
are
V^{11}_{1}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{1\bar{2}}+dz_{\bar{1}2}],[dz_{3\bar{3}}]\rangle
,
V^{11}_{2}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{1\bar{1}}],[dz_{1\bar{2}}-dz_{\bar{1}2}],[dz_{2\bar{2}}]\rangle
,

V^{11}_{3}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{1\bar{3}}],[dz_{2\bar{3}}]\rangle
,
and
V^{11}_{4}=\overline{V^{11}_{3}}
.
The action of
F^{*}
is trivial on
V^{11}_{1}
, and for
j\geq 2
, the action of
F^{*}
on
V_{j}^{11}
is determined by the matrix
A^{11}_{j}
:

A^{11}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}1&-2&1\\
0&1&-1\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad A^{11}_{3}=\begin{pmatrix}u^{3}&-u^{3}\\
0&u^{3}\end{pmatrix},\quad A^{11}_{4}=\overline{A^{11}_{3}}.

Taking real parts of the kernel and cokernel of the restrictions of
F^{*}-\mathrm{Id}
to
V_{j}^{p,q}
, we deduce the expressions for
K^{2}_{k,a}
and
C^{2}_{k,a}
. To compute
K^{3}_{k,a}
and
C^{3}_{k,a}
, we observe that
F^{*}
acts trivially on
H^{3,0}(T^{6}_{k})
and
H^{0,3}(T^{6}_{k})
. The
F^{*}
-invariant subspaces of
H^{2,1}(T^{6}_{k})
are
W_{1}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{1\bar{2}3}+dz_{\bar{1}23}]\rangle
,

W_{2}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{12\bar{3}}]\rangle
,

W_{3}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{13\bar{3}}],[dz_{23\bar{3}}]\rangle
,

W_{4}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{1\bar{1}2}],[dz_{12\bar{2}}]\rangle
, and

W_{5}=\mathbb{C}\langle[dz_{1\bar{1}3}],[dz_{1\bar{2}3}-dz_{\bar{1}23}],[dz_{2\bar{2}3}]\rangle=V^{11}_{2}\wedge\langle[dz_{3}]\rangle
.
The matrix
B_{j}
determines
F^{*}
on
W_{j}
:

B_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}u^{-2}\end{pmatrix},\quad B_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}u^{4}\end{pmatrix},\quad B_{3}=\begin{pmatrix}u&-u\\
0&u\end{pmatrix},\qquad B_{4}=\begin{pmatrix}u&u\\
0&u\end{pmatrix},\qquad B_{5}=u^{-2}A^{1,1}_{2}.

The stated formulas are obtained by taking real parts of the kernel and cokernel of
F^{*}-\mathrm{Id}
on these subspaces.
∎

Remark 21
.

From the proof of Proposition
46
, we deduce that the multiplicity of
1
as an eigenvalue of
F^{*}\colon H^{2}(M_{k,a},\mathbb{R})\to H^{2}(M_{k,a},\mathbb{R})
is
3
. This is because the Jordan form of
A_{2}^{11}
, which determines the restriction of
F^{*}
on the real subspace
\langle i[dz_{1\bar{1}}],i[dz_{1\bar{2}}-dz_{\bar{1}2}],i[dz_{2\bar{2}}]\rangle
, is a
3\times 3
Jordan block with eigenvalue
1
.

Taking into account that
H^{m}(X_{k,a})=H^{m}(M_{k,a})^{\kappa}
,
H^{m}(Z_{k,4})=H^{m}(M_{k,4})^{\langle\kappa,\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle}
and Proposition
17
we obtain:

Theorem 47
.

The manifolds
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{k,a}
,

, in addition:

\displaystyle H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{1}_{1,3})=

\displaystyle H^{3}(\widetilde{Z}_{3,4})=

Therefore, their pairs
(b_{2},b_{3})

are, respectively,

(3,11)
,
(2,10)
,
(4,17)
,
(8,29)
,
(4,9)
, and
(8,13)
.

Proposition
40
and Remark
14
allow us to compute
H^{*}(\widetilde{X}_{k,a})
. The groups
H^{*}(M_{k,a})^{\langle\iota,\kappa\rangle}
are deduced from Proposition
46
. From the description of the singular locus in the proof of Lemma
41
, and Remark

, we obtain that on
M_{1,a}
we have

H^{1}(L_{1})^{-}=\langle[dt]\rangle
, and

when

,

.

Theorem 48
.

Let
\Sigma
be a genus
3
surface. We have
H^{1}(\widetilde{X}^{2}_{k,a})=0
and

\displaystyle H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{2}_{1,a})=

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2}\rangle,

\displaystyle H^{3}(\widetilde{X}^{2}_{1,a})=

\displaystyle\delta^{*}(\langle i[dz_{2\bar{2}}],i[dz_{3\bar{3}}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{2}}]\rangle)\oplus\langle\mathrm{Re}[dz_{123}]\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\langle[dt]\rangle\otimes\mathbf{x}_{1}\oplus\oplus_{j=2}^{4}\langle[dt],[dx_{3}]\rangle\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}\oplus\oplus_{p=1}^{2}H^{1}(\Sigma\times S^{1})\otimes\mathbf{y}_{p},

\displaystyle H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{2}_{2,4})=

\displaystyle\langle\{(\mathrm{Id}+\kappa)^{*}\mathbf{x}_{j}\}_{j=5}^{7}\rangle\oplus\langle\{\mathbf{y}_{p}\}_{p=1}^{4}\rangle,

\displaystyle H^{3}(\widetilde{X}^{2}_{2,4})=

\displaystyle\delta^{*}(\langle i[dz_{2\bar{2}}],i[dz_{3\bar{3}}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{2}}]\rangle)\oplus\langle\mathrm{Re}[dz_{123}],\mathrm{Re}[dz_{12\bar{3}}]\rangle

\displaystyle\oplus\oplus_{j=1,2}\langle[dt]\rangle\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}\oplus\oplus_{j=3}^{4}\langle[dt],[dx_{3}]\rangle\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}

\displaystyle\oplus(\mathrm{Id}+\kappa)^{*}\left(\langle[dt]\rangle\otimes\mathbf{x}_{5}\oplus\oplus_{j=6}^{7}\langle[dt],[dx_{3}],[dy_{3}]\rangle\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\oplus\oplus_{p=1}^{4}H^{1}(\Sigma\times S^{1})\otimes\mathbf{y}_{p}.

The pairs
(b_{2},b_{3})
of
\widetilde{X}_{k,a}^{2}
are, respectively,
(2,25)
if
k=1
and
(7,46)
if
k=2
.

The algebra structure on
H^{*}(\widetilde{X}_{k,a}^{1})
and
H^{*}(\widetilde{Z}_{k,a})
is determined by Proposition
19
. For
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{k,a}
, we need to use it twice. In addition, one needs to compute the Poincaré duals of the components of the singular locus, oriented by the restriction of the
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structure. The rest of this section is devoted to this.

Lemma 49
.

Consider the invariant forms on
M_{2,4}
,
\beta_{1}=\delta^{*}(\mathrm{Im}(dz_{123}))
,
\beta_{2}=\delta^{*}(\mathrm{Im}(dz_{12\bar{3}}))
and
\beta_{3}=dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}}
. Then,

1.

On
X_{2,4}^{1}
,
PD[N_{1}]=PD[N_{2}]=[-\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}]
, and

PD[N_{3}]=PD[N_{4}]=-[\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}].

2.

On
X_{3,4}^{1}
,
PD[N_{j}]=\frac{1}{2}[-\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}],

if
j\leq 4
and
PD[N_{j}]=-\frac{1}{2}[\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}],
if
5\leq j\leq 8
.

3.

On
Z_{2,4}
,

PD[N_{1}]=PD[N_{2}]=2[-\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}]
, and

PD[N_{3}]=PD[N_{4}]=-2[\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}].

4.

On
Z_{3,4}
,

PD[N_{j}]=[-\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}]
, if
j\leq 4
and

PD[N_{j}]=-[\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}]
if
5\leq j\leq 8
.

5.

On
Y_{2,4}
, if
j=1,2,5,8
, and
k=3,4,6,7,9,10
, then

2PD[L_{j}]=PD[L_{k}]=-[\beta_{3}]
.

6.

On
\widetilde{Y}_{2,4}/\widetilde{\kappa}
,

PD[K_{1}]=PD[K_{2}]=2[-\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}]
, and
PD[K_{3}]=PD[K_{4}]=-2[\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}].

Proof.

Proposition
46
implies that
\beta_{1}
,
\beta_{2}
are closed
\langle\iota,\kappa\rangle
-invariant forms on
M_{k,4}
. It is easy to check that
dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}}
satisfies the same properties. Recall that the unit-length volume form of
M_{k,4}
is
-\frac{i}{8}dt\wedge dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}3\bar{3}}
.

Let
X
be one of the orbifolds
X_{2,4}^{1}
,
X_{3,4}^{1}
,
Z_{2,4}
,
Z_{3,4}
,
\widetilde{Y}_{2,4}^{2}/\widetilde{\kappa}
.
Let

[\alpha]\in H^{3}(X)
, then,
[\alpha]=[\delta^{*}\beta+\lambda_{1}\mathrm{Re}(dz_{123})+\lambda_{2}\mathrm{Re}(dz_{12\bar{3}})]
. From

[\alpha\wedge(\mu_{1}\beta_{1}+\mu_{2}\beta_{2})]=\frac{i}{2}(\lambda_{1}\mu_{1}-i\lambda_{2}\mu_{2})[dt\wedge dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}3\bar{3}}],

we obtain

\int_{X}{\alpha\wedge(\mu_{1}\beta_{1}+\mu_{2}\beta_{2})}=(-4\lambda_{1}\mu_{1}+4\lambda_{2}\mu_{2})\mathsf{vol}(X).

Let
K
be a connected component of the singular locus at
t=0
. Since its last coordinate is real (see equations (
56
),(
57
),(
63
)), we have
dz_{123}|_{K}=dz_{12\bar{3}}|_{K}
. Similarly, the last coordinate of a connected component
K^{\prime}
at
t=1/2
is imaginary (see equations (
54
),(
55
),(
62
)) so that
dz_{123}|_{K^{\prime}}=-dz_{12\bar{3}}|_{K^{\prime}}
.
Taking into account that the unit-length volume form of any connected component is the restriction of
\varphi
, which coincides with the restriction of
\mathrm{Re}(dz_{123})
, we obtain

\int_{K}{\alpha}=(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\mathsf{vol}(K),
and
\int_{K^{\prime}}{\alpha}=(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})\mathsf{vol}(K^{\prime}).

Hence

PD[K]=\dfrac{\mathsf{vol}(K)}{4\mathsf{vol}(X)}(-\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}),\qquad PD[K^{\prime}]=\dfrac{-\mathsf{vol}(K^{\prime})}{4\mathsf{vol}(X)}(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}).

The conclusion follows from the values of the volumes. For the orbifolds, we have
1=\mathsf{vol}(M_{2,4})=2\mathsf{vol}(X_{2,4}^{1})=4\mathsf{vol}(\widetilde{Y}_{2,4}/\tilde{\kappa})=8\mathsf{vol}(Z_{2,4})
,
2=\mathsf{vol}(M_{3,4})=2\mathsf{vol}(X_{3,4}^{1})=8\mathsf{vol}(Z_{3,4})
. One can check that all connected components of the singular locus have the same volume:
2
for those on
X_{2,4}^{1}
,
X_{3,4}^{1}
and
\widetilde{Y}_{2,4}/\tilde{\kappa}
, and
1
for those on
Z_{2,4}
and
Z_{3,4}
.

We finally let
[\alpha]\in H^{3}(Y_{2,4})
. Then,

[\alpha]=[\alpha_{1}+\delta^{*}\alpha_{2}]
with
[\alpha_{1}]\in K_{2,4}^{3}
and
\alpha_{2}=\lambda_{1}(dz_{1\bar{2}}+dz_{\bar{1}2})+\lambda_{2}idz_{2\bar{2}}+\lambda_{3}idz_{3\bar{3}}
.
Using that
[\alpha\wedge\beta_{3}]=[i\lambda_{3}dt\wedge dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}3\bar{3}}]
,
\mathsf{vol}(Y_{2,4})=1/2
,
[\alpha]|_{L_{j}}=i\lambda_{3}\delta^{*}[dz_{3\bar{3}}]|_{L_{j}}
and
\varphi|_{L_{j}}=\frac{i}{2}dt\wedge dz_{3\bar{3}}|_{L_{j}}
(which follows from the proof of Lemma
41
), we obtain

\int_{Y_{2,4}}\alpha\wedge\beta_{3}=-4\lambda_{3},\qquad\int_{L_{j}}{\alpha}=2\lambda_{3}\mathsf{vol}(L_{j}).

Hence,
PD[L_{j}]=-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{vol}(L_{j})\beta_{3}
and the statement follows from

\mathsf{vol}(L_{j})=1
if
j=1,2,5,8
, and
\mathsf{vol}(L_{k})=2
if
k=3,4,6,7,9,10
.
∎

Similar computations allow us to prove the next result. For the case of
X_{1,a}
and
\widetilde{Y}_{1,4}/\tilde{\kappa}
, one takes into account that
\mathsf{vol}(X_{1,a})=2\mathsf{vol}(\widetilde{Y}_{1,4}/\tilde{\kappa})=3\sqrt{3}/16
, and
\mathsf{vol}(N_{j})=\mathsf{vol}(K_{j})=3
for
j=1,2
. On
Y_{1,a}
, one uses that
\mathsf{vol}(Y_{1,4})=3\sqrt{3}/16
, and
3\mathsf{vol}(L_{1})=\mathsf{vol}(L_{j})=3\sqrt{3}/2
for
j=2,3,4
.

Lemma 50
.

On
X^{1}_{1,a}
, we have
PD[N_{1}]=PD[N_{2}]=-4/\sqrt{3}\delta^{*}(\mathrm{Im}[dz_{123}])
, on
Y_{1,a}
,
3PD[L_{1}]=PD[L_{j}]=-2[dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}}]
,
j=2,3,4
, and on
\widetilde{Y}_{1,a}/\tilde{\kappa}
,
PD[K_{1}]=PD[K_{2}]=-8/\sqrt{3}\delta^{*}(\mathrm{Im}[dz_{123}])
.

5.3.3
Properties (P2) and (P3)

We first show that the closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structures
\widetilde{\varphi}_{t}
satisfy Property (P2) when
t\to 0
. The first identity follows from directly from equation (
42
), applying it twice for
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{k,a}
. Of course, one needs to take into account that
M_{k,a}
and
M_{k,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle

are finitely covered by a parallelizable manifold (see Remark
15
). Hence,
p_{1}(M_{k,a})=p_{1}(M_{k,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle)=0
. Proposition
26
and Lemmas
49
,
50
allow us to compute the first Pontryagin class:

Corollary 51
.

Let
\widetilde{X}
be one of
\widetilde{X}_{k,a}^{j}
, or
\widetilde{Z}_{k,4}
. Then,

\int_{\widetilde{X}}{p_{1}(\widetilde{X})\wedge\tilde{\varphi}_{t}}<0.

In addition,

\displaystyle p_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{1})=

\displaystyle p_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{1,6}^{1})=8\sqrt{3}\delta^{*}(\mathrm{Im}[dz_{123}]),

\displaystyle p_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{2})=

\displaystyle p_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{1,6}^{2})=14[dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}}]+16\sqrt{3}\delta^{*}(\mathrm{Im}[dz_{123}])-8[\omega_{1}]\otimes\mathbf{y}_{1}-8[\omega_{2}]\otimes\mathbf{y}_{2},

\displaystyle p_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{1})=

\displaystyle p_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1})=12\,\delta^{*}(\mathrm{Im}[dz_{123}]),

\displaystyle p_{1}(\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}^{1})=

\displaystyle p_{1}(\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}^{1})=24\,\delta^{*}(\mathrm{Im}[dz_{123}]),

\displaystyle p_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2})=

\displaystyle 24[dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}}]+24\,\delta^{*}(\mathrm{Im}[dz_{123}])-8\sum_{p=1}^{4}[\omega_{p}]\otimes\mathbf{y}_{p}.

We now verify the second identity in equation (
1
) for
\widetilde{\varphi}_{t}
:

Proposition 52
.

The resolutions
\rho\colon\widetilde{X}^{j}_{k,a}\to{X}^{j}_{k,a}
and
\rho\colon\widetilde{Z}_{k,4}\to{Z}_{k,4}
satisfy:

\int{\alpha^{2}\wedge\widetilde{\varphi}_{t}}<0,\quad\mbox{for every }[\alpha]\in H^{2}.

Proof.

The argument is similar for resolutions done in a single step, as
H^{2}
is spanned by the Thom classes of the connected components of the exceptional divisors, except for
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{1,3}
, where an additional class appears. We therefore focus on this example. Similarly,
\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2}
is the most involved among the rest.

On
\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{1}
,
we let
[\alpha]=\lambda_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathbf{x}_{2}+\lambda_{3}\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{3}}]
, then
[\alpha]^{2}=-2\lambda_{1}^{2}\mathrm{Th}[N_{1}]-2\lambda_{2}^{2}\mathrm{Th}[N_{2}]+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{3}^{2}[dz_{1\bar{1}3\bar{3}}]+2\lambda_{1}\lambda_{3}\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{3}}]\otimes\mathbf{x}_{1}+2\lambda_{2}\lambda_{3}\mathrm{Re}[dz_{1\bar{3}}]\otimes\mathbf{x}_{2}
. Set
y_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
[\theta\wedge\mathrm{Re}(dz_{1\bar{3}})]|_{N_{j}}=y_{j}[\varphi|_{N_{j}}]
. Then,

\displaystyle[\rho^{*}(\varphi)]\cdot[\alpha^{2}]=

\displaystyle-2\lambda_{1}^{2}\rho^{*}(\varphi\wedge\mathrm{Th}[N_{1}])-2\lambda_{2}^{2}\rho^{*}(\varphi\wedge\mathrm{Th}[N_{2}])+\frac{i}{4}\lambda_{3}^{2}\rho^{*}(dt\wedge dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}3\bar{3}}),

\displaystyle[\theta]\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}\cdot[\alpha^{2}]=

\displaystyle-4y_{j}\lambda_{j}\lambda_{3}\rho^{*}(\varphi\wedge\mathrm{Th}[N_{j}]).

Taking into account that the unit-length volume form of
X_{1,3}^{1}
is
-\frac{i}{8}dt\wedge dz_{1\bar{1}2\bar{2}3\bar{3}}
, that
\varphi
calibrates
N_{j}
, and the values of the volumes of
N_{1}
,
N_{2}
,
X_{1,3}^{1}
we get:

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{1}}{\alpha^{2}\wedge\widetilde{\varphi}_{t}}=

\displaystyle-6\lambda_{1}^{2}-6\lambda_{2}^{2}-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{8}\lambda_{3}^{2}+12\pi t^{2}y_{1}\lambda_{3}\lambda_{1}+12\pi t^{2}y_{2}\lambda_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-6\left(\lambda_{1}-{\pi t^{2}y_{1}\lambda_{3}}\right)^{2}-6\left(\lambda_{2}-{\pi t^{2}y_{2}\lambda_{3}}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{8}-6\pi^{2}(y_{1}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})t^{4}\right)\lambda_{3}^{2},

which is negative for all values of
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3})\neq(0,0,0)
when
t\to 0
.

According to Lemma
43
, and the discussion at the end of section
2.4
, on the component
K_{p}=\Sigma_{p}\times S^{1}
of the singular locus of
\widetilde{Y}_{2,4}^{2}/\tilde{\kappa}
we have
[\theta\wedge\omega_{p}]=y_{p}[\varphi|_{K_{p}}]
for some
y_{p}>0
.
Let
[\alpha]=\sum_{j=5}^{7}\lambda_{j}(\mathrm{Id}+\kappa^{*})\mathbf{x}_{j}+\sum_{p=1}^{4}{\mu_{p}\mathbf{y}_{p}}
, and assume
(\lambda_{5},\lambda_{6},\lambda_{7},\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3},\mu_{4})\neq(0,\dots,0)
. Since
\Sigma_{p}
has genus
3
, and
\mathrm{Th}[L_{j}]=\mathrm{Th}[L_{j+3}]
,
j=5,6,7
.

[\alpha]^{2}=-4\sum_{j=5}^{7}\lambda_{j}^{2}(\bar{\rho}_{1}\circ\rho_{2})^{*}\mathrm{Th}[L_{j}]-2\sum_{p=1}^{4}{\mu_{p}^{2}(\rho_{2}^{*}\mathrm{Th}[K_{p}]+4\omega_{p}\otimes\mathbf{y}_{p})}.

(79)

In this case,

[\widetilde{\varphi}_{t_{1},t_{2}}]=[(\bar{\rho}_{1}\circ\rho_{2})^{*}(\varphi)]-\pi t_{1}^{2}\sum_{j=5}^{7}(\mathrm{Id}+\kappa^{*})(\theta_{j}^{1}\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j})-\pi t_{2}^{2}\sum_{p=1}^{4}\theta_{p}^{2}\otimes\mathbf{y}_{p}
.
We have

[\theta_{p}^{2}\otimes\mathbf{y}_{p}]\cdot[\alpha^{2}]=16\mu_{p}^{2}y_{p}\rho_{2}^{*}([\varphi]\wedge\mathrm{Th}[K_{p}])
, and Remark
14
implies
(\mathrm{Id}+\kappa)^{*}[\theta_{j}^{1}]\otimes\mathbf{x}_{j}\cdot[\alpha^{2}]=0
. Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}\wedge\widetilde{\varphi}_{t_{1},t_{2}}}=-\sum_{j=5}^{7}4\lambda_{j}^{2}\mathsf{vol}(L_{j})-\sum_{p=1}^{4}\mu_{p}^{2}(2+16y_{p}\pi t_{2}^{2})\mathsf{vol}(K_{p})<0.

∎

We finally argue that property (P3) holds. From the first three paragraphs of the proof of
[
3
, Proposition 3.1]
, one deduces the following result:

Lemma 53
.

[
3
]

Let
M
be a compact
7
-dimensional manifold with finite fundamental group, and let
\widetilde{M}
be its universal cover.
If
\pi\colon M\to B
is a locally trivial fibration onto a
3
-dimensional base, then
b_{3}(\widetilde{M})\in\{0,1\}
.

From the inequality
b_{3}(\widetilde{M})\geq b_{3}(M)
, Propositions
44
,
45
, and Theorems
47
,
48
, we deduce that the constructed manifolds satisfy (P3).

5.3.4
Formality

We start by proving that

\widetilde{X}_{k,a}^{j}
and
Z_{k,4}
, are the resolution of orbifolds of the form
M/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, where
M
is a non-formal manifold.

Theorem 54
.

The closed
\mathrm{G}_{2}
manifolds
M_{k,a}
,
M_{k,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle
and
\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}
are not formal, and have
b_{1}=1
. Therefore, they do not admit a metric with holonomy contained in
\mathrm{G}_{2}
.

Proof.

Proposition
46
shows that
b_{1}(M_{k,a})=1
. The same holds for
M_{k,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle
because it is the mapping torus of
F_{k,4}\colon T^{6}/\langle\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\rangle\to T^{6}/\langle\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\rangle
.
In addition,
b_{1}(Y_{k,a})=b_{1}(M_{k,a}/\iota\circ\kappa)=1
. To conclude non-formality, we use Theorem
5
(
[
4
, Corollary 16]
). As we have already shown in Proposition
46
and Remark
21
,
(F_{k,a})^{*}
has the eigenvalue
\lambda=1
with multiplicity
3
on
H^{2}(T^{6}_{j,a})
. This shows that
M_{k,a}
is not formal. The manifold
M_{k,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle
is not formal because the subspace where
F_{k,a}^{*}
has eigenvalue
1
with multiplicity
3
in Remark
21
is
invariant under the action of
\langle\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\rangle
.

By Remark
19
,
\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}
is the mapping torus of
\widetilde{F}_{k,a}\colon S_{k}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k}\to S_{k}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k}
,
\widetilde{F}_{k,a}=(\widetilde{f}_{k,a},\mathrm{r}_{a}^{-2})
. In addition,
H^{2}(S_{k}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})=H^{2}(\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\oplus\langle\mathbf{y}_{1},\dots\mathbf{y}_{16}\rangle\oplus H^{2}(\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})
, and
\widetilde{F}_{k,a}
preserves all the summands of this decomposition, restricting to
\widetilde{f}_{k,a}
on
H^{2}(\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k}\times\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{k})
. Using the computations in the proof of Proposition
46
, we deduce that the restriction of
\widetilde{f}_{k,a}
to
\langle idz_{1\bar{1}},i(dz_{1\bar{2}}-dz_{\bar{1}2}),idz_{2\bar{2}}\rangle
is

A^{11}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}1&-2&1\\
0&1&-1\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}.

(80)

This shows that algebraic order of
1
as an eigenvalue of
\widetilde{F}_{k,a}
is greater or equal than
3
. Again,
[
4
, Corollary 16]
ensures
\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}
is non-formal. As shown in
[
18
, Corollary 5.4]
, manifolds with holonomy contained in
\mathrm{G}_{2}
and positive first Betti number are formal. Therefore, these manifolds do not admit torsion-free
\mathrm{G}_{2}
structures.
∎

The remainder of this section is devoted to prove that all the manifolds constructed are formal, except for
\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}
. We start by showing that
\tilde{X}_{1,3}^{1},\widetilde{X}^{1}_{1,6},\widetilde{X}^{2}_{1,3},\widetilde{X}^{2}_{1,6}
are formal using
[
11
, Theorem 1.14]
.

Proposition 55
.

The manifolds
\widetilde{X}^{j}_{1,a}
with
j=1,2
and
a=3,6
are formal.

Proof.

Let
j\in\{1,2\}
,
a=3,6
, then
\widetilde{X}^{j}_{1,a}
satisfies
b_{1}=0
and
b_{2}\leq 3
. In addition, Proposition
52
implies that

(\cdot)\wedge[\widetilde{\varphi}_{t}]\colon H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{j}_{1,a})\to H^{5}(\widetilde{X}^{j}_{1,a})
is an isomorphism. Hence,
\widetilde{X}^{j}_{1,a}
is formal by Proposition
7
(
[
11
, Theorem 1.14]
).
∎

We prove that
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4}
and
\widetilde{X}^{2}_{2,4}
are formal by computing the Bianchi-Massey tensor. Since nonzero-degree maps preserve formality (see
[
31
, Theorem 1]
), this will imply that
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{2,4}
is formal. However, the formality of
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{2,4}
can also be deduced from a direct computation, analogous to the one carried out for
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4}
. In fact, it is simpler in this case because the second Betti number of
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{2,4}
is smaller.

We follow the notations introduced in section
2.2
. To compute
E^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{k,a}^{j})
we take into account Theorems
47
and
48
for the formulas of
H^{2}(\widetilde{X}_{k,a}^{j})
, and Proposition
19
, Remark
14
and Lemma
49
for the product formulas. A basis for
E^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1})
is:

n_{ij}=\mathbf{x}_{i}\odot\mathbf{x}_{j},\quad 1\leq i<j\leq 8,\qquad m_{1j}=\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\odot 2}-\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\odot 2},\quad 2\leq j\leq 4,\qquad m_{5j}=\mathbf{x}_{5}^{\odot 2}-\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\odot 2},\quad 6\leq j\leq 8.

We define
N=\langle n_{ij}\rangle
,
M_{1}=\langle\{m_{1j}\}_{j=2}^{4}\rangle
, and
M_{2}=\langle\{m_{5j}\}_{j=6}^{8}\rangle
.
To obtain a basis of
E^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2})
we denote
\mathbf{y}_{p}=(\mathrm{Id}+\kappa)^{*}(\mathbf{x}_{j})
for
p=5,6,7
. Equation (
79
) and Lemma
49
show that the square of a non-zero element of the form
\sum_{p=5}^{8}\lambda_{p}\mathbf{y}_{p}
is linearly independent to the subspace generated by
\{\mathbf{y}_{p}^{2},\,p=1,2,3,4\}
, which has dimension
4
. A basis of
E^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2})
is:

\displaystyle n_{pq}=\mathbf{y}_{p}\odot\mathbf{y}_{q},\quad 1\leq p<q\leq 7,\quad\qquad m_{5p}=2\mathbf{y}_{5}^{\odot 2}-\mathbf{y}_{p}^{\odot 2},\quad p=6,7.

(81)

We define
N=\langle n_{pq}\rangle
,
M=\langle m_{56},m_{57}\rangle
.

Lemma 56
.

For
(j,k)\in\{(1,3),(2,2)\}
, consider the kernel
\mathcal{B}^{8}(X_{k,4}^{j})
of the full symmetrization tensor
\mathfrak{S}\colon\mathrm{Symm}^{2}(E^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{k,4}^{j}))\to\mathrm{Symm}^{4}(H^{2}(\widetilde{X}_{k,4}^{j}))
,

1.

If
x\in\mathcal{B}^{8}(X_{3,4}^{1})
, then
x
is congruent to

\displaystyle\lambda_{23}m_{12}\odot m_{13}+\lambda_{24}m_{12}\odot m_{14}-(\lambda_{23}+\lambda_{24})m_{13}\odot m_{14}

\displaystyle+\mu_{67}m_{56}\odot m_{57}+\mu_{68}m_{56}\odot m_{58}-(\mu_{67}+\mu_{68})m_{57}\odot m_{58}

modulo
N\odot(N\oplus M_{1}\oplus M_{2})\oplus M_{1}\odot M_{2}
.

2.

If
x\in\mathcal{B}^{8}(X_{2,4}^{2})
, then

x\equiv 0\mod N\odot(N\oplus M)
.

Proof.

On
X_{3,4}^{1}
, the image of
N\odot(N\oplus M_{1}\oplus M_{2})\oplus M_{1}\odot M_{2}
under
\mathfrak{S}
is contained in the set of degree-
4
homogeneous polynomials on the variables

\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{8}
, with degree at most
3
in each variable.
In adittion, if
i=1
and
j,k\in\{2,3,4\}
or
i=5
and
j,k\in\{6,7,8\}
we have:

\mathfrak{S}(m_{ij}\odot m_{ik})=\mathbf{x}_{i}^{4}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{k}^{2}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{j}^{2}+\mathbf{x}_{j}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{k}^{2}.

In particular, the image of the subspace
\langle\{m_{ij}\odot m_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=2,3,4}\cup\{m_{ij}\odot m_{ij}\}_{i=5,j=6,7,8}\rangle
is
6
-dimensional and linearly independent to the image of
N\odot(N\oplus M_{1}\oplus M_{2})\oplus M_{1}\odot M_{2}\oplus\langle\{m_{ij}\odot m_{ik},\,i=1,\,j\neq k\in\{2,3,4\}\mbox{ or }i=5,j\neq k\in\{6,7,8\}\}\rangle
by
\mathfrak{S}
, because the polynomials in that set do not contain any non-zero monomial proportional to
\mathbf{x}_{j}^{4}
with
j=2,3,4,6,7,8
. Hence, if
x\in\mathcal{B}^{8}(X_{3,4}^{1})
then
x
is congruent to

\lambda_{23}m_{12}\odot m_{13}+\lambda_{24}m_{12}\odot m_{14}+\lambda_{34}m_{13}\odot m_{14}\\
+\mu_{67}m_{56}\odot m_{57}+\mu_{68}m_{56}\odot m_{58}+\mu_{78}m_{57}\odot m_{58}

modulo
N\odot(N\oplus M_{1}\oplus M_{2})\oplus M_{1}\odot M_{2}
. The coefficient of
\mathbf{x}_{1}^{4}
in
\mathfrak{S}(x)
is
\lambda_{23}+\lambda_{24}+\lambda_{34}
and that of
\mathbf{x}_{5}^{4}
is
\mu_{67}+\mu_{68}+\mu_{78}
. Since
\mathbf{x}_{1}^{4},\mathbf{x}_{5}^{4}\not\in\mathfrak{S}(N\odot(N\oplus M_{1}\oplus M_{2})\oplus M_{1}\odot M_{2})
, those coefficients should be
0
, as claimed.

Similarly,
\mathfrak{S}
restricted to
N\odot N\oplus N\odot M
is contained in the set of degree-
4
homogeneous polynomials in which each
\mathbf{y}_{i}
appears with degree at most
3
. In addition, if
6\leq p\leq q\leq 7
we have

\mathfrak{S}(m_{5p}\odot m_{5q})=4\mathbf{y}_{5}^{4}-2\mathbf{y}_{5}^{2}\mathbf{y}_{q}^{2}-2\mathbf{y}_{5}^{2}\mathbf{y}_{p}^{2}+\mathbf{y}_{p}^{2}\mathbf{y}_{q}^{2}.

Hence,
\mathfrak{S}(\langle\{m_{5p}\odot m_{5q})\}_{6\leq p\leq q\leq 7}\rangle)
is
3
-dimensional and linearly independent to
\mathfrak{S}(N\odot N\oplus N\odot M)
. The conclusion follows from this.
∎

Lemma
56
allows us to prove that the Bianchi-Massey tensor vanishes on
\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2}
.

Proposition 57
.

\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2}
is formal.

Proof.

Notations as in diagram (
66
). If
1\leq p\leq 4
, we let
\tau_{p}
a Thom forms of the connected component of the exceptional divisor in the resolution
\rho_{2}\colon\widetilde{X}_{k,a}\to\widetilde{Y}_{k,a}/\tilde{\kappa}
determined by
K_{p}
. If
5\leq p\leq 7
, then we pick a
Thom form
\tau_{p}^{\prime}\in\Omega^{2}(\widetilde{Y}_{2,4})
of the connected component of the exceptional divisor of
\rho_{1}\colon\widetilde{Y}_{2,4}\to Y_{2,4}
determined by
L_{p}
. Since the support of
(\mathrm{Id}+\tilde{\kappa})^{*}(\tau_{p}^{\prime})
is disjoint from
\cup_{q=1}^{4}K_{q}
, the form
\tau_{p}=\rho_{2}^{*}(\mathrm{Id}+\tilde{\kappa})^{*}(\tau_{p}^{\prime})
, is smooth. Of course,
\tau_{p}\wedge\tau_{q}=0
if
1\leq p<q\leq 8
.

We define
\alpha\colon H^{2}(\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2})\to\Omega^{2}(\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2})
,

\alpha(\mathbf{y}_{p})=\tau_{p},

1\leq p\leq 7
.
Since
\alpha^{2}(n_{pq})=\tau_{p}\wedge\tau_{q}=0
, there is a homomorphism
\gamma\colon E^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2})\to\Omega^{3}(\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2})
with
d\gamma(e)=\alpha^{2}(e)
such that
\gamma(n_{pq})=0
for every
p<q
. If
x\in\mathcal{B}^{8}(\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2})
, then
x\equiv 0\mod N\odot(N\oplus M)
and
\mathcal{F}_{\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{2}}(x)=0
because

\gamma(n_{jk})\alpha^{2}(n_{pq})=\gamma(n_{jk})\alpha^{2}(m_{5j})=\gamma(m_{pq})\alpha^{2}(n_{ij})=0
. Proposition
7
(
[
11
, Theorem 1.14]
) shows that
\tilde{X}_{2,4}^{2}
is formal.
∎

To conclude formality for
\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1}
, we need to study the configuration of the singular locus. Fix two connected components
N_{j}
and
N_{k}
that lie in the same level set
t_{0}=0,1/2
, we claim that we can find a cobordism
C
contained in
\{t_{0}\}\times T^{6}_{3}
such that
(C_{jk}\cup\kappa(C_{jk}))\cap N_{\ell}=\emptyset
when
\ell\notin\{j,k\}
. For instance, if
t=1/2
, given two different connected components determined by
(n,\varepsilon_{3})
and
(m,\delta_{3})
, the cobordism
C
is the image of

[0,1]\times N_{j}\to M_{3,4}^{1}
,

[t,1/2,n-2iy_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},\varepsilon_{3}+iy_{3}]\longmapsto[1/2,[h_{1}(t)-2iy_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},h_{2}(t)+iy_{3}]],

where
h_{1}(t)=(1-t)n+tm
,
h_{2}(t)=(1-t)\varepsilon_{3}+t\delta_{3}
, and we oriented
C
so that
\partial C=N_{j}\sqcup-N_{k}
.
C
does not intersect the remaining connected components: if
n\neq m
,
then
h_{1}(t)
is an integer when
t=0,1
, and
h_{2}(0)=\varepsilon_{3}
,
h_{4}(1)=\delta_{3}
; otherwise,
(n,(1-t)\varepsilon_{3}+t\delta_{3})\notin\{(1-n,0),(1-n,1/2)\}
.
A similar argument shows that
\kappa(C_{jk})=[1/2,[-h_{1}(t)-2iy_{2},h_{1}(t)+x_{2}-3iy_{2},-h_{2}(t)+iy_{3}]]
is disjoint from the remaining connected components of the singular locus.

There are tubular neighborhoods
U_{jk}
of
C_{jk}
, and
V_{\ell}
of
N_{\ell}
such that if
\ell\neq j,k
, then
U_{jk}\cup\kappa^{*}(U_{jk})\cap V_{\ell}=\emptyset
. Note also that
V_{\ell}
is
\kappa
-invariant as
N_{\ell}\subset\operatorname{Fix}(\kappa)
and
\kappa
is an isometry. We fix
\tau_{j}
a Thom form for
Q_{j}=\rho^{-1}(N_{j})
supported inside a tubular neighborhood of
Q_{j}
contained in
\rho^{-1}(V_{j})
. According to Remark
6
, we can further assume that
\tau_{j}^{2}
vanishes around a smaller tubular neighborhood of
Q_{j}
. In addition, equation (
16
) shows that
-\frac{1}{2}\rho_{*}(\tau_{j}^{2})
is a Thom form of
N_{j}
on
X_{3,4}^{1}
. This allows us to find the maps
\alpha
and
\gamma
.

Proposition 58
.

Define
\alpha\colon H^{2}(\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1})\to\Omega^{2}(\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1})
,
\alpha(\mathbf{x}_{p})=\tau_{p}
. There is

\gamma\colon E^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1})\to\Omega^{3}(\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1})
such that
d\gamma(e)=\alpha^{2}(e)
and

1.

If
p\neq q
,
\alpha^{2}(n_{pq})=0
and
\gamma(n_{pq})=0
.

2.

If
a=1
and
p=2,3,4
or if
a=5
and
p=6,7,8
, then for every
q\neq a
,
q\neq p
we have

\gamma(m_{ap})\wedge\alpha(\mathbf{x}_{q})=0
.

Proof.

Since
\tau_{p}\wedge\tau_{q}=0
for
p\neq q
, we set
\gamma(n_{pq})=0
.
We now define
\gamma(m_{ap})
for
a=1
and
p=2,3,4
or
a=5
and
p=6,7,8
. By Lemma
8
, given
N_{a^{\prime}}\neq N_{p}
a component that is cobordant to
N_{p}
, then we can find
\beta^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}p}
supported on
U_{a^{\prime}p}\cup V_{a^{\prime}}\cup V_{p}
such that
d\beta^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}p}=-\frac{1}{2}\rho_{*}(\tau_{a^{\prime}}^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{*}(\tau_{p}^{2})
. Then,
-(\beta_{a^{\prime}p}^{\prime}+\kappa^{*}\beta_{a^{\prime}p}^{\prime})
is a
\kappa
-invariant primitive of
\rho_{*}(\tau_{a^{\prime}}^{2})-\rho_{*}(\tau_{p}^{2})
and it is supported on

U_{a^{\prime}p}\cup\kappa(U_{a^{\prime}p})\cup V_{a^{\prime}}\cup V_{p}
. Indeed, by Lemma
18
(
[
30
, Lemma 5]
), we can assume that
\beta_{a^{\prime}p}=-\rho^{*}(\beta_{a^{\prime}p}^{\prime}+\kappa^{*}\beta_{a^{\prime}p}^{\prime})
is smooth. In particular,
\rho^{*}(\beta_{a^{\prime}p})\wedge\tau_{q}=0
if
q\neq a^{\prime},p
. The choice
\gamma(m_{ap})=\beta_{ap}
satisfies the announced properties.
∎

Remark 22
.

Given two cobordant connected components
N_{a}^{\prime}
and
N_{p}
, we obtained primitives
{\beta}_{a^{\prime}p}
of
\tau_{a^{\prime}}^{2}-\tau_{p}^{2}
with
{\beta}_{a^{\prime}p}\wedge\tau_{q}=0
when
q\neq a^{\prime},p
and such that
\rho_{*}({\beta}_{a^{\prime}p})
is well-defined.

Proposition 59
.

The manifolds
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4}
and
\widetilde{X}^{1}_{2,4}
are formal.

Proof.

Using Proposition
7
(
[
11
, Theorem 1.14]
) and
[
31
, Theorem 1]
, it suffices to show that

\mathcal{F}_{\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4}}=0
. If
x\in\mathcal{B}(\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4})
, then
x
is congruent to

\displaystyle\lambda_{23}m_{12}\odot m_{13}+\lambda_{24}m_{12}\odot m_{14}-(\lambda_{23}+\lambda_{24})m_{13}\odot m_{14}

\displaystyle+\mu_{67}m_{56}\odot m_{57}+\mu_{68}m_{56}\odot m_{58}-(\mu_{67}+\mu_{68})m_{57}\odot m_{58}

modulo
N\odot(N\oplus M_{1}\oplus M_{2})\oplus M_{1}\odot M_{2}
by Lemma
56
. Let
\alpha
and
\gamma
be the maps obtained in Proposition
58
. Using its properties, we deduce that
\mathcal{F}_{\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4}}=0
on
N\odot(N\oplus M_{1}\oplus M_{2})\oplus M_{1}\odot M_{2}
. Denoting
\gamma(m_{ap})=\beta_{ap}
, and using
\beta_{ap}\wedge\tau_{q}=0
for
q\neq a,p
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\widetilde{X}^{1}_{3,4}}(x)=

\displaystyle[\lambda_{23}\beta_{12}\wedge(\tau_{1}^{2}-\tau_{3}^{2})+\lambda_{24}\beta_{12}\wedge(\tau_{1}^{2}-\tau_{4}^{2})-(\lambda_{23}+\lambda_{24})\beta_{13}\wedge(\tau_{1}^{2}-\tau_{4}^{2})]

\displaystyle+[\mu_{67}\beta_{56}\wedge(\tau_{5}^{2}-\tau_{7}^{2})+\mu_{68}\beta_{56}\wedge(\tau_{5}^{2}-\tau_{8}^{2})-(\mu_{67}+\mu_{68})\beta_{57}\wedge(\tau_{5}^{2}-\tau_{8}^{2})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda_{23}+\lambda_{24})[(\beta_{12}-\beta_{13})\wedge\tau_{1}^{2}]+(\mu_{67}+\mu_{68})[(\beta_{56}-\beta_{57})\wedge\tau_{5}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda_{23}+\lambda_{24})[(\beta_{12}-\beta_{13})\wedge(\tau_{1}^{2}-\tau_{4}^{2})]+(\mu_{67}+\mu_{68})[(\beta_{56}-\beta_{57})\wedge(\tau_{5}^{2}-\tau_{8}^{2})],

It suffices to show that
[(\beta_{12}-\beta_{13})\wedge(\tau_{1}^{2}-\tau_{4}^{2})]=[(\beta_{56}-\beta_{57})\wedge(\tau_{5}^{2}-\tau_{8}^{2})]=0
. The proofs of both identities are similar, so we conclude by verifying that

\int_{\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1}}{(\beta_{12}-\beta_{13})\wedge(\tau_{1}^{2}-\tau_{4}^{2})}=0
. Similar to the proof of Proposition
20
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{X}_{3,4}^{1}}{(\beta_{12}-\beta_{13})\wedge(\tau_{1}^{2}-\tau_{4}^{2})}=

\displaystyle 8\int_{M_{3,4}}{\rho_{*}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{12}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{13}\right)\wedge\rho_{*}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\tau_{4}^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\mathrm{lk}_{M_{3,4}}(N_{3}\sqcup-N_{2},N_{1}\sqcup-N_{4}),

because
-\frac{1}{2}\rho_{*}(\tau_{1}^{2}-\tau_{4}^{2})
is a Thom form for
N_{1}\sqcup-N_{4}
,
-\frac{1}{2}d(\rho_{*}(\beta_{12}-\beta_{13}))=-\frac{1}{2}\rho_{*}(\tau_{3}^{2}-\tau_{2}^{2})
, and
-\frac{1}{2}\rho_{*}(\tau_{3}^{2}-\tau_{2}^{2})
is a Thom form for
N_{3}\sqcup-N_{2}
.

The form
-\beta_{23}
constructed in Remark
22
, pushes forward to
M
, and it satisfies
d(-\beta_{23})=\tau_{3}^{2}-\tau_{2}^{2}
, and
\beta_{23}\wedge\tau_{1}=\beta_{23}\wedge\tau_{4}=0
. Hence,

\mathrm{lk}_{M_{3,4}}(N_{3}\sqcup-N_{2},N_{1}\sqcup-N_{4})=\frac{1}{4}\int_{M_{3,4}}\rho_{*}\beta_{23}\wedge\rho_{*}(\tau_{1}^{2}-\tau_{4}^{2})=0.

∎

Utilizing Proposition
20
, we
finally show that
\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}
is not formal, and hence, by
[
31
, Theorem 1]
, neither is
\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}
.
First of all,
from equations (
54
), (
62
) we observe
that a component determined by
\operatorname{Fix}(\eta_{2})
is not homologous to a component determined by
\operatorname{Fix}(\eta\circ\zeta_{2})
on
T^{6}_{2}
(compare for instance, their Poincaré duals). However, we now show that these are homologous on
M_{2,4}
.

Proposition 60
.

There is an oriented cobordism
C\subset M_{2,4}
, with boundary on the projection of
\{1/2\}\times T^{6}_{2}
such that
[\partial C]+[\partial(\kappa(C))]=2[N_{3}^{1}]-2[N_{4}^{1}]
on
H_{3}(\{1/2\}\times T^{6}_{2},\mathbb{Z})
.

For every
j,k=1,2
we have
C\cap N_{j}^{k}=\{p_{j}^{k}\}
and the intersection is negative at every
p_{1}^{k}
and positive at every
p_{2}^{k}
.

Proof.

Equation (
54
) shows that
N_{3}^{1}
is the image of the embedding

(\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z})^{3}\to M_{2,4},\quad[x_{2},y_{2},y_{3}]\mapsto[1/2,[-2iy_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},iy_{3}]].

The pullback of
\varphi|_{N_{3}^{1}}
is
-2dx_{2}\wedge dy_{2}\wedge dy_{3}
, i.e., an oriented frame for
TN_{3}^{1}
is
(\partial_{x_{2}},2\partial_{y_{1}}-\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}})
. We now view
N_{4}^{1}
in the level set
t=-1/2
;
using equations (
55
) and
[1/2,[-2x_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},-1/4+iy_{3}]]=[-1/2,[i2x_{2},y_{2}+ix_{2},1/4-iy_{3}]]
, and performing a change of variables, we deduce that
N_{4}^{1}
is the image of

(\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z})^{3}\to M_{2,4},\quad[x_{2},y_{2},y_{3}]\mapsto[-1/2,[2iy_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},1/4+iy_{3}]].

The pullback of
\varphi|_{N_{3}^{1}}
is
2dx_{2}\wedge dy_{2}\wedge dy_{3}
, and an oriented frame for
TN_{4}^{1}
is
(\partial_{x_{2}},2\partial_{y_{1}}+\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}})
. Define the embedding
\Psi\colon[-1/2,1/2]\times N_{4}^{1}\to M_{2,4}
,

\Psi(t,[1/2,[2iy_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},1/4+iy_{3}]])=[t,[2iy_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},h(t)+iy_{3}]],

where
h=0
on
[-1/2,1/8]
and
h=1/4
on
[1/4,1/2]
. We orient
C=\mathrm{Im}(\Psi)
by the frame:

(\psi_{*}(\partial_{t}),\psi_{*}(\partial_{x_{2}}),\psi_{*}(\partial_{y_{2}}),\psi_{*}(\partial_{y_{3}}))=(\partial_{t}+h^{\prime}(t)\partial_{x_{3}},\partial_{x_{2}},2\partial_{y_{1}}+\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}}).

According to the orientations described in section
2.3
,
\partial C=-N_{4}^{1}\sqcup L_{1}
, where
L_{1}=\Psi(\{1/2\}\times N_{4}^{1})
is oriented by
(\partial_{x_{2}},2\partial_{y_{1}}+\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}})
. Since

\kappa[t,[2iy_{2},x_{2}+iy_{2},h(t)+iy_{3}]]=[1-t,[2iy_{2},x_{2}-3iy_{2},-h(t)+iy_{3}]],\qquad 1-t\in[1/2,3/2],

the orientation induced by
\kappa
in
\kappa(C)
is
(-\partial_{t}-h^{\prime}(t)\partial_{x_{3}},\partial_{x_{2}},2\partial_{y_{1}}-3\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}})
. Note that
\partial\kappa(C)=\kappa(\partial C)
. Since
\kappa|_{N_{4}^{1}}=\mathrm{Id}
, we obtain that
\partial\kappa(C)=-N_{4}^{1}\sqcup\kappa(L_{1})
, where
L_{2}=\kappa(L_{1})
is oriented by the restriction of
(\partial_{x_{2}},2\partial_{y_{1}}-3\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}})
when its viewed on the level set
t=1/2
.
This shows that
[\partial C]+[\partial\kappa(C)]=-2[N_{4}^{1}]+[L_{1}]+[L_{2}]
on
H_{3}(T^{6}_{2},\mathbb{Z})
. We now claim that
[L_{1}]+[L_{2}]=2[N_{3}^{1}]
on
H_{3}(T^{6},\mathbb{Z})
. To verify this, we rewrite
T^{6}_{2}=T^{2}_{1}\times T^{2}_{2}\times T^{2}_{3}
, where
T^{2}_{1}
,
T^{2}_{2}
and
T_{2}^{3}
are determined respectively by the variables
(y_{1},y_{2})
,
(x_{2},y_{3})
and
(x_{1},x_{3})
. Note that
(\partial_{y_{1}},\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}},\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{3}})
is a positive frame when
T_{2}^{6}
is endowed with the standard orientation.
Define
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}\subset T^{2}_{1}
as the (oriented) image of the embeddings
e_{j}\colon\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}\to T^{2}_{1}
,

e_{1}[x]=[2x,x],\quad e_{2}[x]=[2x,-3x],\quad e_{3}[x]=[2x,-x].

Orienting
T_{2}^{2}
by
(\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}})
, we obtain
A_{j}\times T^{2}_{2}\times\{[0,0]\}
, is
-L_{j}
if
j=1,2
and
-N_{3}^{1}
otherwise. The claimed equality follows from
[A_{1}]+[A_{2}]-2[A_{3}]=0
on
H_{1}(T^{2}_{1},\mathbb{Z})
.

Regarding the intersections, we have
C\cap N_{j}^{k}=\{p_{j}^{k}\}
, where
p_{j}^{k}=[0,0,0,\varepsilon_{j}^{k}]
and
\varepsilon_{1}^{1}=0
,
\varepsilon_{1}^{2}=1/2

\varepsilon_{2}^{1}=-1/4
, and
\varepsilon_{2}^{2}=1/4
. We now compute their sign. At the level set
t=0
, the metric restricts to the standard flat metric on
T^{6}_{2}
, and an
oriented frame for
\nu(C)|_{t=0}
is
(\partial_{x_{1}},-\partial_{y_{1}}+2\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})
. In addition, taking into account expressions (
56
), (
63
) and the formula of
\varphi
, we obtain that an oriented frame of
TN_{1}^{k}
is
(\partial_{x_{1}}+\partial_{y_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}}-\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})
, and an oriented frame for
TN_{2}^{k}
is
(\partial_{x_{1}}-\partial_{y_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}}+\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})
. Hence, an oriented frame for
\nu(N_{j}^{k})
is

(-\partial_{t},\partial_{x_{1}}+(-1)^{j}\partial_{y_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}}+(-1)^{j+1}\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}})
. As explained in section
2.3
, the orientation on
C\cap N_{j}^{k}=\{p_{j}^{k}\}
is determined by comparing those of
TM|_{\{p_{j}^{k}\}}
and
\nu(C)|_{p_{j}^{k}}\oplus\nu(N_{j}^{k})|_{p_{j}^{k}}
, where the latter is endowed with the direct sum orientation. The statement follows from the equality:

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\wedge(-\partial_{y_{1}}+2\partial_{y_{2}})\wedge\partial_{x_{3}}\wedge(-\partial_{t})\wedge(\partial_{x_{1}}+(-1)^{j}\partial_{y_{1}})\wedge(\partial_{x_{2}}+(-1)^{j+1}\partial_{y_{2}})\wedge\partial_{y_{3}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\wedge\partial_{x_{1}}\wedge 2\partial_{y_{2}}\wedge\partial_{x_{3}}\wedge((-1)^{j}\partial_{y_{1}})\wedge\partial_{x_{2}}\wedge\partial_{y_{3}}=(-1)^{j}2\,\partial_{t}\wedge\partial_{x_{1}}\wedge\partial_{y_{1}}\wedge\partial_{x_{2}}\wedge\partial_{y_{2}}\wedge\partial_{x_{3}}\wedge\partial_{y_{3}}.

∎

Using Lemma
8
we construct a primitive of a representative of
\mathrm{Th}[N_{3}^{1}]-\mathrm{Th}[N_{4}^{1}]
.

Proposition 61
.

For
j=3,4
, and
k=1,2
, let
\upsilon_{j}^{k}
be a Thom form of
N_{j}^{k}
. There is
\beta\in\Omega^{3}(M_{2,4})
such that
d\beta=\upsilon_{3}^{1}-\upsilon_{4}^{1}
, and for every
j,k=1,2
and for every choice of Thom form
\upsilon_{j}^{k}
of
N_{j}^{k}
we have,

\int_{M_{2,4}}{\beta\wedge\upsilon_{j}^{k}}=(-1)^{j}.

Proof.

Since
[\partial C]+[\partial\kappa(C)]=2[N_{3}]-2[N_{4}]
on
\{1/2\}\times T^{6}_{2}
, Lemma
9
allows us to construct
Thom forms
\tau_{\partial C}
,
\tau_{\partial\kappa(C)}
,
\tau_{3}^{1}
, and
\tau_{4}^{1}
of
\partial C
,
\partial\kappa(C)
,
N_{3}^{1}
and
N_{4}^{1}
, and

\alpha_{1}\in\Omega^{3}(M_{2,4})
supported on
(1/2-\varepsilon,1/2+\varepsilon)\times T^{6}_{2}
with
d\alpha_{1}=\tau_{\partial C}+\tau_{\partial\kappa(C)}-2\tau_{3}+2\tau_{4}
. Lemma
8
provides us with
\beta_{C}\in\Omega^{3}(M_{2,4})
such that
d\beta_{C}=\tau_{\partial C}
.

Note that
\kappa^{*}(\partial_{C})
is a Thom form of
\partial\kappa(C)
because
\kappa
preserves the orientation of
M
, and we chose the orientation of
\kappa(C)
so that
\kappa\colon C\to\kappa(C)
is orientation-preserving. Hence, there is
\alpha_{2}\in\Omega^{3}(M_{2,4})
supported near
\partial\kappa(C)
that satisfies
d\alpha_{2}=\tau_{\partial\kappa(C)}-\kappa^{*}(\tau_{\partial C})
. We also choose
\alpha_{3}
and
\alpha_{4}
supported near
N_{3}^{1}
and
N_{4}^{1}
such that
d\alpha_{j}=\upsilon_{j}^{1}-\tau_{j}
,
j=3,4
. Therefore,

d\beta=\upsilon_{3}^{1}-\upsilon_{4}^{1},\qquad\beta=\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta_{C}+\kappa^{*}(\beta_{C})-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\right)+\alpha_{3}-\alpha_{4}.

Let
j,k=1,2
, and let
\upsilon_{j}^{k}
be a Thom form of
N_{j}^{k}
, then

\beta\wedge\upsilon_{j}^{k}=\frac{1}{2}(\beta_{C}+\kappa^{*}\beta_{C})\wedge\upsilon_{j}^{k}
. Lemma
8
ensures
[\beta_{C}\wedge\upsilon_{j}^{k}]=[\beta_{C}\wedge\kappa^{*}(\upsilon_{j}^{k})]=\mathrm{Th}[C\cap N_{j}^{k}]
, since
\kappa^{*}(\upsilon_{j}^{k})
is also a Thom form of
N_{j}^{k}
, as
\kappa
preserves the orientations of both
M_{2,4}
and
N_{j}^{k}
. Using Proposition
60
, and that
\kappa
preserves the orientation of
M_{2,4}
, we obtain:

\int_{M_{2,4}}{\beta\wedge\upsilon_{j}^{k}}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{M_{2,4}}{\beta_{C}\wedge(\upsilon_{j}^{k}+\kappa^{*}(\upsilon_{j}^{k}))}=(-1)^{j}.

∎

Proposition 62
.

Let
\rho\colon\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}\to Z_{2,4}
be the resolution map and
Q_{j}=\rho^{-1}(N_{j})
.
Then,
\mathrm{lk}_{M_{2,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle}(N_{1}\sqcup-N_{2},N_{3}\sqcup-N_{4})\neq 0
and the triple Massey product

\langle\mathrm{Th}[Q_{3}]+\mathrm{Th}[Q_{4}],\mathrm{Th}[Q_{1}]+\mathrm{Th}[Q_{2}],\mathrm{Th}[Q_{1}]-\mathrm{Th}[Q_{2}]\rangle,

on
\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}
does not vanish. Both
\widetilde{Z}_{2,4}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}
are not formal.

Proof.

To compute the linking number (see Definition
10
), we
consider Thom forms
\upsilon_{j}\in\Omega^{4}(M_{2,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle)
of
N_{j}
with
j=1,2,3,4
, and we now find a primitive
\alpha\in\Omega^{3}(M_{2,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle)
of
\upsilon_{3}-\upsilon_{4}
.
Let
q\colon M_{2,4}\to M_{2,4}^{\prime}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle
be the quotient map, then
q^{*}\upsilon_{j}
is a
\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle
-invariant
Thom form of
N_{j}^{1}\sqcup N_{j}^{2}
, and we write

q^{*}(\upsilon_{j})=\upsilon_{j}^{1}+\upsilon_{j}^{2}
, where
\upsilon_{j}^{k}
are Thom forms for
N_{j}^{k}
. Using Proposition
61
, we pick
\beta\in\Omega^{3}(M_{2,4})
such that
d\beta=\upsilon_{3}^{1}-\upsilon_{4}^{1}
and
\int_{M_{2,4}}{\beta\wedge\bar{\upsilon}_{j}^{k}}=(-1)^{j}
for every
j,k=1,2
and
\bar{\upsilon}_{j}^{k}
Thom form of
N_{j}^{k}
.

Recall that for
j=1,2
, the involution
\mathrm{j}_{1}
fixes
N_{j}^{k}
, whereas for
j=3,4
, it swaps
N_{j}^{1}
with
N_{j}^{2}
. From the
\mathrm{j}_{1}
-invariance of
q^{*}\upsilon_{j}
it follows
\mathrm{j}_{1}^{*}(\upsilon_{j}^{k})=\upsilon_{j}^{k}
for
j,k=1,2
and
\mathrm{j}_{1}^{*}(\upsilon_{j}^{1})=\upsilon_{j}^{2}
for
j=3,4
. Using also that
\mathrm{j}_{1}
preserves the orientation, we deduce that
\beta^{1}=\beta+\mathrm{j}_{1}^{*}\beta\in\Omega^{3}(M_{2,4})
satisfies

d\beta^{1}=q^{*}\upsilon_{3}-q^{*}\upsilon_{4},\quad\int_{M_{2,4}}{\beta^{1}\wedge\upsilon_{j}^{k}}=2\int_{M_{2,4}}{\beta\wedge\upsilon_{j}^{k}}=(-1)^{j}2,\qquad j,k=1,2.

Consider the
\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle
-invariant form
\alpha^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}(\beta^{1}+\mathrm{j}_{2}^{*}\beta^{1})
. Similarly, using that
\mathrm{j}_{2}
is orientation-preserving and fixes
q^{*}\upsilon_{j}
, we obtain

d\alpha^{\prime}=q^{*}\upsilon_{3}-q^{*}\upsilon_{4},\quad\int_{M_{2,4}}{\alpha^{\prime}\wedge q^{*}(\upsilon_{j})}=\int_{M_{2,4}}{\beta^{1}\wedge q^{*}(\upsilon_{j})}=(-1)^{j}4,\qquad j=1,2.

Let
\alpha=q_{*}(\alpha^{\prime})
. Then

d\alpha=\upsilon_{3}-\upsilon_{4}
, and

\mathrm{lk}(N_{1}\sqcup-N_{2},N_{3}\sqcup-N_{4})=\int_{M_{2,4}/\langle\mathrm{j}_{1},\mathrm{j}_{2}\rangle}{\alpha\wedge(\upsilon_{1}-\upsilon_{2})}=\frac{1}{4}\int_{M_{2,4}}{\alpha^{\prime}\wedge q^{*}(\upsilon_{1}-\upsilon_{2})}=\frac{1}{4}(-4-4)=-2.

The conclusion follows from Proposition
20
, Lemma
6
(
[
13
, Proposition 2.90]
), and
[
31
, Theorem 1]
.
∎

Remark 23
.

A similar argument shows that
\widetilde{Z}_{3,4}
has a non-vanishing triple Massey product.

5.4
Comparison with known compact holonomy
\mathrm{G}_{2}
manifolds

We now argue that, among the constructed manifolds only
\widetilde{X}_{1,6}^{2}
shares the triple
(\pi_{1}(\bullet),b_{2}(\bullet),b_{3}(\bullet))
with the holonomy
\mathrm{G}_{2}
manifolds found in
[
10
,
21
,
22
,
23
,
24
,
25
,
26
,
27
,
30
,
36
]
.

In Joyce’s cited works, the pair
(b_{2},b_{3})=(3,11)
appears in
[
22
, Example 8]
, which match those of
\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{1}
. However, his example is simply connected and
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{X}_{1,3}^{1})=\mathbb{Z}_{3}
. Similarly, a simply connected example with
(b_{2},b_{3})=(4,17)
appears in
[
22
, Example 11]
, but the manifold constructed here with those Betti numbers, namely
\widetilde{X}_{2,4}^{1}
, has fundamental group
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. As mentioned, there are two simply connected manifolds with
(b_{2},b_{3})=(2,10)
in
[
22
, Examples 13 and 14]
, but constructed in a different way from
\widetilde{X}_{1,6}^{1}
. The first is the quotient of
\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}/\Lambda
, where
\Lambda
is constructed using a
7
th root of unity, by the dihedral group
D_{7}
. The second
is the resolution of the orbit space of
\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}\times(\mathbb{C}/\Gamma_{1})^{3}
under the action of a group of order
18
that contains
D_{3}
.
As for the remaining pairs of
(b_{2},b_{3})
, namely

(2,25),(4,9),(7,46),(8,13),(8,29)
, in
[
24
, Figure 1, Tables 12.1–12.7, sections 12.2,12.3, 12.8]
and
[
23
, Figure 12.3]
we observe that there is not any holonomy
\mathrm{G}_{2}
manifold with
b_{2}=2
and
20\leq b_{3}\leq 30
, or
b_{2}=4
and
b_{3}\leq 16
, or
b_{2}=7
, and
45\leq b_{3}\leq 47
,
b_{2}=8
and
b_{3}\leq 18
, or
b_{2}=8
and
24\leq b_{3}\leq 30
.

The examples found by Kovalev in
[
26
]
have
0\leq b_{2}\leq 1
. Kovalev and Lee found manifolds with
b_{2}=2,8
in
[
27
, Table 1]
, but these have
b_{3}\geq 53
.
Their examples with
b_{2}=3
have
b_{3}\geq 58
, and those with
b_{2}=7
have
b_{3}\geq 62
(see discussion after Proposition 6.1).
All the examples constructed by Corti, Haskins, Nordström and Pacini
[
10
]
are simply connected (see Theorem 4.9), and most are
2
-connected. The positive values of
b_{2}
that occur are
b_{2}=3,20
, so there is no overlap with our examples. The examples by Nordström in
[
32
]
are
2
-connected, except for one, which has
b_{2}=1
.
The new example by Joyce and Karigiannis
[
25
, Example 7.3]
has
b_{2}=0
. The compact holonomy
\mathrm{G}_{2}
manifolds in
[
36
]
are simply connected and they do not have
b_{2}=2
or
b_{2}=7
. Examples with
b_{2}=4
and
b_{2}=8
appear in Theorems 4.5 and 4.8. Those with
b_{2}=4
have
b_{3}\geq 27
and those with
b_{2}=8
have
b_{3}\geq 31
. Finally, the non-formal example in
[
30
]
has
b_{2}=11
.
